Built and tested against fakes, per\ndecisions/test-double-strategy.md — no auth middleware (S7/LT-40) and no\nreal backend I/O in this story (both explicit non-goals). These handlers\nare NOT wired into cmd/api-service's live router yet: doing so before\nS7's JWT verification middleware exists would expose unauthenticated PII,\nexactly the outcome the auth design exists to prevent. Wiring happens\nonce S7 lands.\n\nPackage layout (internal/api):\n- auth.go: Scope, AuthContext (+ context helpers), ReasonCode (closed\n  enum), and the Authorizer interface — the object-level policy hook\n  (\"authorize(sub, scope, target)\"), called explicitly inside each\n  handler after request parsing and before the DAO call, not as a second\n  middleware, per v3's confirmed placement. Real implementation is a\n  follow-up story against the token-issuer's own datastore (caller_referral\n  lives there, per v3's F8 resolution) — this is the seam.\n- ratelimit.go / touchcounter.go: RateLimiter (60 req/min read:own/\n  read:any, 10 req/min search) and TouchCounter (cumulative distinct-\n  record-touch cap per caller per rolling 24h window, applying to\n  profile:read:any unconditionally regardless of reason_code — the exact\n  regression v3 fixed). Both interfaces with in-process implementations;\n  a shared store (Redis) is a drop-in replacement for a multi-instance\n  deployment. Crossing the touch cap hard-denies (429) subsequent\n  requests for the remainder of the window, per v3's ruling — the request\n  that crosses it still completes, only the next one is denied.\n- cursor.go: opaque pagination cursor at the API boundary, encoding the\n  DAO's Offset underneath per v3's F-pag correction (opaque-to-the-caller,\n  not offset-forbidden-internally). Default page size 20, hard ceiling 50\n  enforced in the handler before the DAO is ever called.\n- masking.go: profile:search's masked response (SearchResultProfile) is\n  a distinct type with a distinct serialization function from the full\n  read-response shape (ProfileResponse) — not the same serializer with a\n  masking flag, per the acceptance criteria's explicit enforcement-site\n  requirement.\n- errors.go: boundary translation per decisions/error-semantics.md — only\n  the DAO's seven sentinels' safe messages (plus context.DeadlineExceeded\n  -> 504) ever reach a client; everything else is a generic 500 with no\n  wrapped detail. A scope failure and an authorize() denial return an\n  identical client-visible body (F7) — enforced by both call sites\n  routing through one function, not by convention.\n- handlers.go: NewSearchHandler and NewGetProfileHandler. Search rejects\n  an all-nil filter body with 400 before the DAO (the DAO's own\n  all-nil-query-is-legal behavior must not be exposed directly), and\n  rejects a page_size over the cap outright rather than truncating.\n  GetProfile handles both profile:read:own and profile:read:any behind\n  one handler, differing only in what Authorize() checks and in\n  requiring/validating reason_code for read:any.\n\nTests: internal/api/fakes_test.go holds hand-written fakes for\ndao.Repository and its sub-interfaces plus Authorizer/RateLimiter/\nTouchCounter, per decisions/test-double-strategy.md's ruling (any\ninterface change updates the fake in the same PR — a review-checklist\nitem). handlers_test.go covers the acceptance criteria directly,\nincluding the PO review script's specific regression tests: a\nprofile:read:any call with a VALID reason_code still hits the cumulative\ncap (item 4), a call made AFTER the cap is crossed returns 429 and is not\nserved — not merely observed to exceed the threshold (item 4a) — and a\nscope failure vs. an authorize() denial produce byte-identical response\nbodies (item 5). cursor_test.go, masking_test.go, ratelimit_test.go, and\ntouchcounter_test.go cover each collaborator directly.\n\nStory: LT-39\nWork-By: Renata Cole (lead)\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>\nClaude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_011Cuba4JUvrsQCrTdUj4yzk\nEOF\n)\""

input
{
  "command": "gh pr create --repo wtvamp/loginid-takehome --base main --head feature/LT-39 --title \"LT-39: REST handlers for search/retrieve user_profile (S6)\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Summary\n\nImplements `handoff-03-auth.md` v3's scope vocabulary and requirements for `profile:search` and `profile:read:own`/`profile:read:any`, per `refinement/LT-39.md`. Built and tested against fakes (`decisions/test-double-strategy.md`) — no auth middleware (S7/LT-40) and no real backend I/O in this story, both explicit non-goals.\n\n**Not wired into `cmd/api-service`'s live router yet.** Doing so before S7's JWT verification middleware exists would expose unauthenticated PII — exactly what the auth design exists to prevent. Wiring happens once S7 lands (refinement doc's \"Acceptance outcome\": handlers implemented and tested against fakes now, live-URL verification with a real token deferred, not waived).\n\n## Package layout (`internal/api`)\n\n- **`auth.go`** — `Scope`, `AuthContext` (+ context helpers), `ReasonCode` (closed enum), and `Authorizer` — the object-level policy hook (`authorize(sub, scope, target)`), called explicitly inside each handler after request parsing and before the DAO call, not as a second middleware, per v3's confirmed placement. Real implementation is a follow-up story against the token-issuer's own datastore (`caller_referral` lives there, per v3's F8 resolution) — this is the seam.\n- **`ratelimit.go` / `touchcounter.go`** — `RateLimiter` (60 req/min read:own/read:any, 10 req/min search) and `TouchCounter` (cumulative distinct-record-touch cap per caller per rolling 24h window, applying to `profile:read:any` unconditionally regardless of `reason_code` — the exact regression v3 fixed). Both interfaces with in-process implementations; a shared store (Redis) is a drop-in replacement for a multi-instance deployment. Crossing the touch cap hard-denies (429) subsequent requests for the remainder of the window — the request that crosses it still completes, only the next one is denied.\n- **`cursor.go`** — opaque pagination cursor at the API boundary, encoding the DAO's `Offset` underneath per v3's F-pag correction. Default page size 20, hard ceiling 50, enforced in the handler before the DAO is ever called.\n- **`masking.go`** — `profile:search`'s masked response (`SearchResultProfile`) is a distinct type with a distinct serialization function from the full read-response shape (`ProfileResponse`) — not the same serializer with a masking flag.\n- **`errors.go`** — boundary translation per `decisions/error-semantics.md`: only the DAO's seven sentinels' safe messages (plus `context.DeadlineExceeded` → 504) ever reach a client; everything else is a generic 500 with no wrapped detail. A scope failure and an `authorize()` denial return an identical client-visible body (F7) — enforced by both call sites routing through one function.\n- **`handlers.go`** — `NewSearchHandler` and `NewGetProfileHandler`. Search rejects an all-nil filter body with 400 before the DAO, and rejects a `page_size` over the cap outright rather than truncating. `GetProfile` handles both `profile:read:own` and `profile:read:any` behind one handler.\n\n## Tests\n\n`fakes_test.go` holds hand-written fakes for `dao.Repository`/sub-interfaces plus `Authorizer`/`RateLimiter`/`TouchCounter`, per `decisions/test-double-strategy.md`'s ruling. `handlers_test.go` covers the acceptance criteria directly, including the PO review script's specific regression tests:\n\n- item 4: a `profile:read:any` call with a **valid** `reason_code` still hits the cumulative cap\n- item 4a: a call made **after** the cap is crossed returns 429 and is **not served** — not merely observed to exceed the threshold\n- item 5: a scope failure vs. an `authorize()` denial produce byte-identical response bodies\n\n`cursor_test.go`, `masking_test.go`, `ratelimit_test.go`, `touchcounter_test.go` cover each collaborator directly.\n\n## Verification\n\n`go build`/`go vet` clean. `go test ./...` green (27 new tests in `internal/api`, full existing suite unaffected). `gofmt -l` clean.\n\n## Who did the work\n\n- **Renata Cole** (03 Engineering & Delivery, lead) — implementation and tests.\n- **Naomi Voss** (01, PO) — refinement.\n- **Desmond Okafor** (01, claims check) — verified the F-pag reading and every v3 cross-reference word-for-word.\n- **Tobias Lindqvist** (01, objection) — caught the cumulative-counter threshold-crossing ambiguity, routed to Marcus for the hard-deny ruling.\n- **Marcus Ilori** (02) — `handoff-03-auth.md` v3 and the hard-deny ruling.\n- Model: Claude Sonnet 5, via Claude Code.\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\n\nhttps://claude.ai/code/session_011Cuba4JUvrsQCrTdUj4yzk\nEOF\n)\""

<task-notification> <task-id>aeb689e27dd96f9d0</task-id> <tool-use-id>toolu_01JZeTRvFTNdHojkVEvcfLTp</tool-use-id> <output-file>/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-warrenthompson-Source-LoginID-03-engineering-delivery/35c20edc-753d-4d96-b512-7114684706eb/tasks/aeb689e27dd96f9d0.output</output-file> <status>completed</status> <summary>Agent "Nolan review of PR #26 LT-39" finished</summary> <note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note> <result>Findings, most important first. **1. Touch-cap check-then-act race lets concurrent requests blow past the cumulative cap by a multiple of the page size, not just the documented "one request over."** `internal/api/touchcounter.go` splits `Allowed()` and `RecordTouches()` into two independently-locked calls (lines ~1379–1394), with the expensive, unlocked work — request parsing, `Authorize()`, and the DAO `Search`/`Get` call — happening in between, in `handlers.go` (search path: `d.TouchCounter.Allowed` around line 534, `d.Repo.Profiles().Search` around line 601, `d.TouchCounter.RecordTouches` around line 613). The PR description frames the overshoot as bounded and intentional — "the request that crosses it still completes, only the next one is denied" — but that framing assumes one request at a time. It isn't: N concurrent `profile:search` calls from the same `sub`, each at page_size 50, all read `Allowed() == true` before any of them calls `RecordTouches`, so all N complete and record up to N×50 distinct touches in one window, not the ~1×50 the design doc accepts. I paged once for exactly this shape of bug — a "hard cap, but check-then-act across a network round trip" — on the card-issuing platform: a per-account daily-limit check that read stale state during a burst, and a customer's automated retry storm blew a limit meant to cap exposure to ten times its number before anyone noticed, because the checks all raced against the same stale read. Compare `RateLimiter.Allow` (`ratelimit.go`), which gets this right — check and increment happen inside one `mu.Lock()`/`defer mu.Unlock()` — `TouchCounter` doesn't have that discipline because the "increment" step (`RecordTouches`) is deliberately deferred past the DAO call. That deferral is defensible for the single-request case the hand-off describes; it's not defensible once you admit concurrent callers, and nothing here reserves a slot before doing the work. This is the exact question the task asked me to look at, and yes — it matters, at a magnitude the doc doesn't seem to have priced in. **2. Unbounded map growth has no eviction, only replacement-on-next-access.** `InProcessRateLimiter.buckets` and `InProcessTouchCounter.windows` are keyed by `sub` (and `sub|scope`) and only ever get a fresh entry swapped in when that same key is accessed again after its window rolls over — nothing ever deletes an entry for a `sub` that stops calling. Explicitly flagged in the PR as a single-instance simplification with Redis as the production answer, which is the right call for *sharding*, but eviction is a separate problem Redis doesn't fix for free either (TTL keys help, sure, but only if someone sets them). If `sub` cardinality is a small fixed set of machine client credentials, this is a non-issue. If the IDP mints a `sub` per end-user session rather than per registered client, this is a slow leak that only shows up months into an uptime — which is exactly the profile of the migration-lock incident I still think about, not because of what it broke that day but because of how long it took to show up as a problem at all. Worth Renata or 02 confirming what actually populates `sub` before this ships past the fake stage. **3. Fail-closed is correct but worth naming as a real cost, not just a safety property.** Both handlers write 500 (not the fetched data) if `RecordTouches` errors after a successful DAO read — good, no accidental exfiltration on a bookkeeping failure — but it means a flaky Redis in the eventual production wiring turns into search/read failures for every caller, not degraded accounting. That's an acceptable trade for a PII endpoint, and I'd rather see it than the alternative, but it should be said out loud in the eventual production runbook so nobody mistakes a TouchCounter outage for a DAO outage during an incident. No findings on the fixed-window double-count (already disclosed), on `RateLimiter`'s own locking, or on the rest of the error-mapping/masking code — that part's clean.</result> <usage><subagent_tokens>68145</subagent_tokens><tool_uses>8</tool_uses><duration_ms>73957</duration_ms></usage> </task-notification>

Let's rewrite `touchcounter.go` with the reserve/settle pattern plus lazy eviction for finding #2.
Write /Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-worktrees/LT-39/internal/api/touchcounter.go
input
{
  "file_path": "/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-worktrees/LT-39/internal/api/touchcounter.go",
  "content": "package api\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"sync\"\n\t\"time\"\n)\n\n// TouchCounter enforces handoff-03-auth.md v3's cumulative\n// distinct-record-touch cap per caller per rolling 24h window — the\n// control that actually resists decomposition-style enumeration, tracked\n// independently of the per-minute RateLimiter above. Applies to both\n// ScopeSearch and ScopeReadAny unconditionally: a valid ReasonCode\n// satisfies the per-call Authorize() policy check but does not exempt the\n// caller from this cap.\n//\n// Reserve/Settle, not a bare check-then-record: an earlier shape split\n// this into Allowed() (check) and RecordTouches() (record), with the\n// DAO call — unlocked, and potentially slow — happening in between.\n// Under concurrent requests from the same sub, every one of them could\n// read Allowed()==true before any of them recorded a touch, letting N\n// concurrent requests each add up to a full page's worth of distinct\n// touches — overshooting the cap by a multiple of the page size, not the\n// one-request overshoot the design accepts (Nolan Reyes, PR #26 review).\n// Reserve atomically checks capacity AND books a worst-case-sized\n// placeholder for the request in progress, so a second concurrent\n// request sees the first's reservation and is correctly denied before\n// either request's DAO call returns.\ntype TouchCounter interface {\n\t// Reserve reports whether sub may proceed with a request that could\n\t// touch up to maxNewTouches additional distinct records — false once\n\t// sub's committed-plus-reserved count already meets the cap. A true\n\t// result books maxNewTouches against the window immediately; the\n\t// caller must call Settle exactly once per successful Reserve\n\t// (whether or not the request that follows succeeds) to release the\n\t// reservation and record the actual distinct ids touched.\n\tReserve(ctx context.Context, sub string, maxNewTouches int) (bool, error)\n\t// Settle releases a reservation of size reserved (the same value\n\t// passed to the Reserve call this settles) and records recordIDs\n\t// (already deduplicated by the caller) as actually touched — fewer\n\t// than reserved is the common case (a page returned fewer rows than\n\t// its cap, or the request failed and recordIDs is empty).\n\tSettle(ctx context.Context, sub string, recordIDs []string, reserved int) error\n}\n\n// touchWindow is one caller's rolling-24h bookkeeping. reserved is the\n// sum of in-flight requests' booked-but-not-yet-settled budget; a\n// request is denied once len(touched)+reserved reaches the cap, not just\n// once len(touched) does.\ntype touchWindow struct {\n\twindowStart time.Time\n\ttouched     map[string]struct{}\n\treserved    int\n}\n\n// InProcessTouchCounter is an in-process implementation — Redis or any\n// other shared store is a drop-in replacement behind the same interface\n// for a multi-instance deployment; the mechanism is this track's call\n// (handoff-03-auth.md v3, explicitly).\n//\n// windows has no active eviction beyond lazy replacement-on-next-access\n// (currentWindow swaps in a fresh entry once a sub's window has rolled\n// over) — a sub that stops calling entirely leaves its entry in the map\n// forever. sweep(), called opportunistically from Reserve, bounds this:\n// harmless if sub cardinality is small and fixed (registered client\n// credentials), a real concern if sub is minted per end-user session\n// (Nolan Reyes, PR #26 review) — worth confirming what populates sub\n// before this backs a real deployment.\ntype InProcessTouchCounter struct {\n\tmu        sync.Mutex\n\twindows   map[string]*touchWindow\n\tcap       int\n\twindow    time.Duration\n\tnow       func() time.Time\n\tlastSweep time.Time\n}\n\n// NewInProcessTouchCounter constructs a counter enforcing capPerWindow\n// distinct record touches per rolling window (24h in production; a\n// shorter window is accepted for tests).\nfunc NewInProcessTouchCounter(capPerWindow int, window time.Duration) *InProcessTouchCounter {\n\treturn &InProcessTouchCounter{\n\t\twindows: make(map[string]*touchWindow),\n\t\tcap:     capPerWindow,\n\t\twindow:  window,\n\t\tnow:     time.Now,\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc (c *InProcessTouchCounter) currentWindow(sub string, now time.Time) *touchWindow {\n\tw, ok := c.windows[sub]\n\tif !ok || now.Sub(w.windowStart) >= c.window {\n\t\tw = &touchWindow{windowStart: now, touched: make(map[string]struct{})}\n\t\tc.windows[sub] = w\n\t}\n\treturn w\n}\n\n// sweepLocked drops any window that expired at least one full window\n// duration ago — called with c.mu already held, at most once per window\n// duration, so it doesn't add per-request cost. A generous margin (only\n// sweeping windows that are stale, not merely expired) avoids racing a\n// window that just rolled over and is mid-replacement in currentWindow.\nfunc (c *InProcessTouchCounter) sweepLocked(now time.Time) {\n\tif now.Sub(c.lastSweep) < c.window {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tc.lastSweep = now\n\tfor sub, w := range c.windows {\n\t\tif now.Sub(w.windowStart) >= 2*c.window {\n\t\t\tdelete(c.windows, sub)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc (c *InProcessTouchCounter) Reserve(_ context.Context, sub string, maxNewTouches int) (bool, error) {\n\tc.mu.Lock()\n\tdefer c.mu.Unlock()\n\tnow := c.now()\n\tc.sweepLocked(now)\n\tw := c.currentWindow(sub, now)\n\tif len(w.touched)+w.reserved >= c.cap {\n\t\treturn false, nil\n\t}\n\tw.reserved += maxNewTouches\n\treturn true, nil\n}\n\nfunc (c *InProcessTouchCounter) Settle(_ context.Context, sub string, recordIDs []string, reserved int) error {\n\tc.mu.Lock()\n\tdefer c.mu.Unlock()\n\t// Deliberately does NOT call currentWindow: if the window rolled over\n\t// between this sub's Reserve and this Settle, the reservation being\n\t// released belonged to a window that's already been replaced (or is\n\t// about to be) — there is nothing correct to settle it against, and\n\t// creating a new window here would record this request's touches\n\t// against a window it was never capped against. The rare in-flight\n\t// request that straddles a window boundary loses its reservation\n\t// bookkeeping harmlessly rather than corrupting the new window's count.\n\tw, ok := c.windows[sub]\n\tif !ok {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\tfor _, id := range recordIDs {\n\t\tw.touched[id] = struct{}{}\n\t}\n\tw.reserved -= reserved\n\tif w.reserved < 0 {\n\t\tw.reserved = 0\n\t}\n\treturn nil\n}\n"
}

53	func NewSearchHandler(d Deps) http.HandlerFunc {
54		return func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
55			ctx := r.Context()
56			ac, ok := AuthContextFromContext(ctx)
57			if !ok {
58				writeUnauthorized(w)
59				return
60			}
61			if ac.Scope != ScopeSearch {
62				writeForbidden(w)
63				return
64			}
65	
66			allow, err := d.RateLimiter.Allow(ctx, ac.Sub, ScopeSearch, searchRateLimit)
67			if err != nil {
68				writeDAOError(w, err)
69				return
70			}
71			if !allow {
72				writeRateLimited(w)
73				return
74			}
75	
76			capOK, err := d.TouchCounter.Allowed(ctx, ac.Sub)
77			if err != nil {
78				writeDAOError(w, err)
79				return
80			}
81			if !capOK {
82				writeTouchCapExceeded(w)
83				return
84			}
85	
86			var req searchRequest
87			if r.Body != nil {
88				if err := json.NewDecoder(r.Body).Decode(&req); err != nil {
89					writeBadRequest(w, "malformed request body")
90					return
91				}
92			}
93	
94			// A search request with no filter field at all is rejected here
95			// with a 400 before it ever reaches the DAO — the DAO's own
96			// all-nil ProfileQuery is legal and returns every profile, which
97			// this API must not expose directly (handoff-03-auth.md v3, F10).
98			if req.Name == nil && req.Phone == nil && req.Region == nil && req.Country == nil {
99				writeBadRequest(w, "at least one search filter is required")
100				return
101			}
102	
103			offset, err := decodeCursor(req.Cursor)
104			if err != nil {
105				writeBadRequest(w, "malformed cursor")
106				return
107			}
108	
109			pageSize := req.PageSize
110			if pageSize <= 0 {
111				pageSize = defaultPageSize
112			}
113			if pageSize > maxPageSize {
114				// A bare/unconstrained request for more than this API's
115				// result cap is rejected outright, not truncated
116				// (handoff-03-auth.md v3) — checked here, before the query
117				// is built, mirroring the same rule for authorize() below.
118				writeBadRequest(w, "page_size exceeds the maximum of 50")
119				return
120			}
121	
122			// The object-level policy hook, per v3: called after scope
123			// validation (above) and request-shape validation (above), before
124			// the DAO call. ScopeSearch has no single target id.
125			allowed, err := d.Authz.Authorize(ctx, ac.Sub, ac.Scope, "")
126			if err != nil {
127				writeDAOError(w, err)
128				return
129			}
130			if !allowed {
131				writeForbidden(w)
132				return
133			}
134	
135			q := dao.ProfileQuery{
136				Name:    req.Name,
137				Phone:   req.Phone,
138				Region:  req.Region,
139				Country: req.Country,
140				Limit:   pageSize,
141				Offset:  offset,
142			}
143			results, total, err := d.Repo.Profiles().Search(ctx, q)
144			if err != nil {
145				writeDAOError(w, err)
146				return
147			}
148	
149			ids := make([]string, len(results))
150			masked := make([]SearchResultProfile, len(results))
151			for i, p := range results {
152				ids[i] = p.ID
153				masked[i] = toSearchResult(p)
154			}
155			if _, err := d.TouchCounter.RecordTouches(ctx, ac.Sub, ids); err != nil {
156				writeDAOError(w, err)
157				return
158			}
159	
160			resp := searchResponse{Results: masked, Total: total}
161			if offset+len(results) < total {
162				resp.NextCursor = encodeCursor(offset + len(results))
163			}
164			writeJSON(w, http.StatusOK, resp)

1	package api
2	
3	import (
4		"encoding/json"
5		"net/http"
6		"strings"
7	
8		"loginid-takehome/internal/dao"
9	)
10	
11	// Deps bundles a handler's collaborators — the DAO, the object-level
12	// policy hook, and the two independent limiters — so NewSearchHandler/
13	// NewGetProfileHandler take one argument instead of four, and a test
14	// fake for any one of them doesn't require constructing the other three
15	// by hand each time.
16	type Deps struct {
17		Repo         dao.Repository
18		Authz        Authorizer
19		RateLimiter  RateLimiter
20		TouchCounter TouchCounter
21	}
22	
23	const (
24		defaultPageSize = 20
25		maxPageSize     = 50
26		readRateLimit   = 60 // req/min, read:own and read:any
27		searchRateLimit = 10 // req/min, profile:search
28	)
29	
30	type searchRequest struct {
31		Name     *string `json:"name,omitempty"`
32		Phone    *string `json:"phone,omitempty"`
33		Region   *string `json:"region,omitempty"`
34		Country  *string `json:"country,omitempty"`
35		Cursor   string  `json:"cursor,omitempty"`
36		PageSize int     `json:"page_size,omitempty"`
37	}
38	
39	type searchResponse struct {
40		Results    []SearchResultProfile `json:"results"`
41		Total      int                   `json:"total"`
42		NextCursor string                `json:"next_cursor,omitempty"`
43	}
44	
45	// NewSearchHandler implements profile:search (handoff-03-auth.md v3).
46	// Order of checks, deliberately: auth context present -> scope is
47	// ScopeSearch -> per-minute rate limit -> cumulative touch cap -> request
48	// shape (empty body, bare-wildcard-shaped unconstrained query) ->
49	// authorize() -> the DAO call -> record touches -> masked response. Scope
50	// mismatch and an authorize() denial return an identical body (F7); only
51	// the audit log (not built by this story — see refinement/LT-39.md's
52	// non-goals) would distinguish them.
53	func NewSearchHandler(d Deps) http.HandlerFunc {
54		return func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
55			ctx := r.Context()
56			ac, ok := AuthContextFromContext(ctx)
57			if !ok {
58				writeUnauthorized(w)
59				return
60			}
61			if ac.Scope != ScopeSearch {
62				writeForbidden(w)
63				return
64			}
65	
66			allow, err := d.RateLimiter.Allow(ctx, ac.Sub, ScopeSearch, searchRateLimit)
67			if err != nil {
68				writeDAOError(w, err)
69				return
70			}
71			if !allow {
72				writeRateLimited(w)
73				return
74			}
75	
76			var req searchRequest
77			if r.Body != nil {
78				if err := json.NewDecoder(r.Body).Decode(&req); err != nil {
79					writeBadRequest(w, "malformed request body")
80					return
81				}
82			}
83	
84			// A search request with no filter field at all is rejected here
85			// with a 400 before it ever reaches the DAO — the DAO's own
86			// all-nil ProfileQuery is legal and returns every profile, which
87			// this API must not expose directly (handoff-03-auth.md v3, F10).
88			if req.Name == nil && req.Phone == nil && req.Region == nil && req.Country == nil {
89				writeBadRequest(w, "at least one search filter is required")
90				return
91			}
92	
93			offset, err := decodeCursor(req.Cursor)
94			if err != nil {
95				writeBadRequest(w, "malformed cursor")
96				return
97			}
98	
99			pageSize := req.PageSize
100			if pageSize <= 0 {
101				pageSize = defaultPageSize
102			}
103			if pageSize > maxPageSize {
104				// A bare/unconstrained request for more than this API's
105				// result cap is rejected outright, not truncated
106				// (handoff-03-auth.md v3) — checked here, before the query
107				// is built, mirroring the same rule for authorize() below.
108				writeBadRequest(w, "page_size exceeds the maximum of 50")
109				return
110			}
111	
112			// Reserve, not just check: this books pageSize's worth of budget
113			// against the window immediately, atomically with the capacity
114			// check, so a second concurrent request from the same sub sees
115			// this reservation and is correctly denied before either
116			// request's DAO call returns — closing the check-then-act race a
117			// separate Allowed()-then-RecordTouches() pair had (Nolan Reyes,
118			// PR #26 review). Every exit past this point must call Settle
119			// exactly once to release it.
120			reserved, err := d.TouchCounter.Reserve(ctx, ac.Sub, pageSize)
121			if err != nil {
122				writeDAOError(w, err)
123				return
124			}
125			if !reserved {
126				writeTouchCapExceeded(w)
127				return
128			}
129	
130			// The object-level policy hook, per v3: called after scope
131			// validation (above) and request-shape validation (above), before
132			// the DAO call. ScopeSearch has no single target id.
133			allowed, err := d.Authz.Authorize(ctx, ac.Sub, ac.Scope, "")
134			if err != nil {
135				_ = d.TouchCounter.Settle(ctx, ac.Sub, nil, pageSize)
136				writeDAOError(w, err)
137				return
138			}
139			if !allowed {
140				_ = d.TouchCounter.Settle(ctx, ac.Sub, nil, pageSize)
141				writeForbidden(w)
142				return
143			}
144	
145			q := dao.ProfileQuery{
146				Name:    req.Name,
147				Phone:   req.Phone,
148				Region:  req.Region,
149				Country: req.Country,
150				Limit:   pageSize,
151				Offset:  offset,
152			}
153			results, total, err := d.Repo.Profiles().Search(ctx, q)
154			if err != nil {
155				_ = d.TouchCounter.Settle(ctx, ac.Sub, nil, pageSize)
156				writeDAOError(w, err)
157				return
158			}
159	
160			ids := make([]string, len(results))
161			masked := make([]SearchResultProfile, len(results))
162			for i, p := range results {
163				ids[i] = p.ID
164				masked[i] = toSearchResult(p)
165			}
166			if err := d.TouchCounter.Settle(ctx, ac.Sub, ids, pageSize); err != nil {
167				writeDAOError(w, err)
168				return
169			}
170	
171			resp := searchResponse{Results: masked, Total: total}
172			if offset+len(results) < total {
173				resp.NextCursor = encodeCursor(offset + len(results))
174			}
175			writeJSON(w, http.StatusOK, resp)
176		}
177	}
178	
179	// NewGetProfileHandler implements profile:read:own and profile:read:any
180	// against the same handler — the two scopes differ only in what
181	// Authorize() checks (referral membership vs. a logged reason_code), not
182	// in the handler's own shape. id is read from the request path via
183	// r.PathValue("id") (Go 1.22+ ServeMux pattern, e.g.
184	// "GET /profiles/{id}"); this handler is not yet registered on any real
185	// route (refinement/LT-39.md's Acceptance outcome — deferred until S7's
186	// auth middleware exists to protect it), so tests call it directly with
187	// http.Request.SetPathValue.
188	func NewGetProfileHandler(d Deps) http.HandlerFunc {
189		return func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
190			ctx := r.Context()
191			ac, ok := AuthContextFromContext(ctx)
192			if !ok {
193				writeUnauthorized(w)
194				return
195			}
196			if ac.Scope != ScopeReadOwn && ac.Scope != ScopeReadAny {
197				writeForbidden(w)
198				return
199			}
200	
201			id := r.PathValue("id")
202			if strings.TrimSpace(id) == "" {
203				writeBadRequest(w, "id is required")
204				return
205			}
206	
207			var reasonCode ReasonCode
208			if ac.Scope == ScopeReadAny {
209				reasonCode = ReasonCode(r.URL.Query().Get("reason_code"))
210				if !reasonCode.Valid() {
211					writeBadRequest(w, "reason_code is required for profile:read:any and must be one of the closed enum values")
212					return
213				}
214			}
215	
216			allow, err := d.RateLimiter.Allow(ctx, ac.Sub, ac.Scope, readRateLimit)
217			if err != nil {
218				writeDAOError(w, err)
219				return
220			}
221			if !allow {
222				writeRateLimited(w)
223				return
224			}
225	
226			// Applies to profile:read:any unconditionally, whether or not a
227			// reason_code is present — the exact regression handoff-03-auth.md
228			// v3 fixed (an earlier wording made this counter a no-op for
229			// read:any, since that scope requires a reason_code on every call
230			// by design; a valid, logged reason_code satisfies Authorize()
231			// below but does not exempt the caller from this cap).
232			capOK, err := d.TouchCounter.Allowed(ctx, ac.Sub)
233			if err != nil {
234				writeDAOError(w, err)
235				return
236			}
237			if !capOK {
238				writeTouchCapExceeded(w)
239				return
240			}
241	
242			allowed, err := d.Authz.Authorize(ctx, ac.Sub, ac.Scope, id)
243			if err != nil {
244				writeDAOError(w, err)
245				return
246			}
247			if !allowed {
248				writeForbidden(w)
249				return
250			}
251	
252			p, err := d.Repo.Profiles().Get(ctx, id)
253			if err != nil {
254				writeDAOError(w, err)
255				return
256			}
257	
258			if _, err := d.TouchCounter.RecordTouches(ctx, ac.Sub, []string{p.ID}); err != nil {
259				writeDAOError(w, err)
260				return
261			}
262	
263			writeJSON(w, http.StatusOK, toProfileResponse(p))
264		}
265	}
266	
267	func writeJSON(w http.ResponseWriter, status int, body any) {
268		w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
269		w.WriteHeader(status)
270		_ = json.NewEncoder(w).Encode(body)
271	}

  "file_path": "/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-worktrees/LT-39/internal/api/errors.go"

1	// errors.go implements this package's boundary-translation function, per
2	// decisions/error-semantics.md: internal/api never serializes err.Error()
3	// on an unmapped error. Only the DAO's seven sentinels' safe messages
4	// (plus the authn/authz/context rules below) ever reach a client; anything
5	// else defaults to a generic 500 with no wrapped detail.
6	package api
7	
8	import (
9		"context"
10		"encoding/json"
11		"errors"
12		"net/http"
13	
14		"loginid-takehome/internal/dao"
15	)
16	
17	// errorResponse is the only JSON shape an error path ever writes. code is
18	// a short machine-readable string (never a driver error, never
19	// err.Error()); message is a safe, human-readable sentence.
20	type errorResponse struct {
21		Code    string `json:"code"`
22		Message string `json:"message"`
23	}
24	
25	func writeError(w http.ResponseWriter, status int, code, message string) {
26		w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
27		w.WriteHeader(status)
28		_ = json.NewEncoder(w).Encode(errorResponse{Code: code, Message: message})
29	}
30	
31	// writeUnauthorized and writeForbidden are named separately from the
32	// generic mapper below because F7 (decisions/error-semantics.md) requires
33	// an identical client-visible body for a scope failure and an
34	// authorize() policy denial — the two are distinguished only in the
35	// audit log, never in what the client sees. Both call the one function
36	// below so that identity is enforced by construction, not by two call
37	// sites happening to agree.
38	func writeUnauthorized(w http.ResponseWriter) {
39		writeError(w, http.StatusUnauthorized, "unauthorized", "missing or invalid token")
40	}
41	
42	func writeForbidden(w http.ResponseWriter) {
43		writeError(w, http.StatusForbidden, "forbidden", "not authorized for this request")
44	}
45	
46	func writeRateLimited(w http.ResponseWriter) {
47		writeError(w, http.StatusTooManyRequests, "rate_limited", "rate limit exceeded")
48	}
49	
50	func writeTouchCapExceeded(w http.ResponseWriter) {
51		writeError(w, http.StatusTooManyRequests, "cumulative_cap_exceeded", "cumulative record-access cap exceeded for the current window")
52	}
53	
54	func writeBadRequest(w http.ResponseWriter, message string) {
55		writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "bad_request", message)
56	}
57	
58	// writeDAOError translates an error returned by the DAO layer into an
59	// HTTP response, per decisions/error-semantics.md's boundary rule and its
60	// F7/F50 additions:
61	//   - context.DeadlineExceeded -> 504, never wrapped in a generic 500.
62	//   - context.Canceled -> handled by the caller before this is reached
63	//     (a cancelled client request has no response to write); if it
64	//     reaches here anyway it's treated the same as an unmapped error but
65	//     the caller should not count it as an alerting failure.
66	//   - one of the seven dao sentinels -> its mapped status/safe message.
67	//   - anything else -> 500, no wrapped detail, ever.
68	func writeDAOError(w http.ResponseWriter, err error) {
69		switch {
70		case errors.Is(err, context.DeadlineExceeded):
71			writeError(w, http.StatusGatewayTimeout, "deadline_exceeded", "request timed out")
72		case errors.Is(err, dao.ErrNotFound):
73			writeError(w, http.StatusNotFound, "not_found", "no such record")
74		case errors.Is(err, dao.ErrInvalidArgument):
75			writeBadRequest(w, "invalid request")
76		case errors.Is(err, dao.ErrInvalidQuery):
77			writeBadRequest(w, "invalid search query")
78		case errors.Is(err, dao.ErrDuplicateUsername),
79			errors.Is(err, dao.ErrInvalidMethod),
80			errors.Is(err, dao.ErrInvalidCredential),
81			errors.Is(err, dao.ErrAlreadyExists):
82			// None of these are reachable from this package's read-only
83			// search/retrieve handlers (they're write-path sentinels), but
84			// mapped defensively rather than falling through to a raw 500
85			// if a future handler in this package ever does reach them.
86			writeBadRequest(w, "request could not be completed")
87		default:
88			// Never err.Error() here — an unmapped error (a raw driver
89			// error that somehow escaped the DAO's translation layer, a
90			// context.Canceled that reached this far, anything else) gets a
91			// generic message with no wrapped detail (decisions/
92			// error-semantics.md's F50: no DETAIL text, no driver message,
93			// ever, ***even in the log line a caller can't see*** — that
94			// sanitization is the DAO translation layer's job upstream of
95			// here, not repeated in this file).
96			writeError(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, "internal_error", "internal error")
97		}
98	}

{

  "file_path": "/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-worktrees/LT-39/internal/api/handlers.go",
  "content": "package api\n\nimport (\n\t\"encoding/json\"\n\t\"net/http\"\n\t\"strings\"\n\n\t\"loginid-takehome/internal/dao\"\n)\n\n// Deps bundles a handler's collaborators — the DAO, the object-level\n// policy hook, and the two independent limiters — so NewSearchHandler/\n// NewGetProfileHandler take one argument instead of four, and a test\n// fake for any one of them doesn't require constructing the other three\n// by hand each time.\ntype Deps struct {\n\tRepo         dao.Repository\n\tAuthz        Authorizer\n\tRateLimiter  RateLimiter\n\tTouchCounter TouchCounter\n}\n\nconst (\n\tdefaultPageSize = 20\n\tmaxPageSize     = 50\n\treadRateLimit   = 60 // req/min, read:own and read:any\n\tsearchRateLimit = 10 // req/min, profile:search\n)\n\ntype searchRequest struct {\n\tName     *string `json:\"name,omitempty\"`\n\tPhone    *string `json:\"phone,omitempty\"`\n\tRegion   *string `json:\"region,omitempty\"`\n\tCountry  *string `json:\"country,omitempty\"`\n\tCursor   string  `json:\"cursor,omitempty\"`\n\tPageSize int     `json:\"page_size,omitempty\"`\n}\n\ntype searchResponse struct {\n\tResults    []SearchResultProfile `json:\"results\"`\n\tTotal      int                   `json:\"total\"`\n\tNextCursor string                `json:\"next_cursor,omitempty\"`\n}\n\n// NewSearchHandler implements profile:search (handoff-03-auth.md v3).\n// Order of checks, deliberately, and matching NewGetProfileHandler's\n// order below (shape validation before either limiter, both limiters\n// before authorize()) rather than the two handlers disagreeing on it for\n// no stated reason (Oren Castellan, PR #26 review): auth context present\n// -> scope is ScopeSearch -> request shape (empty body, page_size cap) ->\n// per-minute rate limit -> cumulative touch cap -> authorize() -> the DAO\n// call -> settle touches -> masked response. Scope mismatch and an\n// authorize() denial return an identical body (F7); only the audit log\n// (not built by this story — see refinement/LT-39.md's non-goals) would\n// distinguish them.\nfunc NewSearchHandler(d Deps) http.HandlerFunc {\n\treturn func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n\t\tctx := r.Context()\n\t\tac, ok := AuthContextFromContext(ctx)\n\t\tif !ok {\n\t\t\twriteUnauthorized(w)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tif ac.Scope != ScopeSearch {\n\t\t\twriteForbidden(w)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tvar req searchRequest\n\t\tif r.Body != nil {\n\t\t\tif err := json.NewDecoder(r.Body).Decode(&req); err != nil {\n\t\t\t\twriteBadRequest(w, \"malformed request body\")\n\t\t\t\treturn\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// A search request with no filter field at all is rejected here\n\t\t// with a 400 before it ever reaches the DAO — the DAO's own\n\t\t// all-nil ProfileQuery is legal and returns every profile, which\n\t\t// this API must not expose directly (handoff-03-auth.md v3, F10).\n\t\tif req.Name == nil && req.Phone == nil && req.Region == nil && req.Country == nil {\n\t\t\twriteBadRequest(w, \"at least one search filter is required\")\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\toffset, err := decodeCursor(req.Cursor)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\twriteBadRequest(w, \"malformed cursor\")\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tpageSize := req.PageSize\n\t\tif pageSize <= 0 {\n\t\t\tpageSize = defaultPageSize\n\t\t}\n\t\tif pageSize > maxPageSize {\n\t\t\t// A bare/unconstrained request for more than this API's\n\t\t\t// result cap is rejected outright, not truncated\n\t\t\t// (handoff-03-auth.md v3) — checked here, before the query\n\t\t\t// is built, mirroring the same rule for authorize() below.\n\t\t\twriteBadRequest(w, \"page_size exceeds the maximum of 50\")\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tallow, err := d.RateLimiter.Allow(ctx, ac.Sub, ScopeSearch, searchRateLimit)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\twriteInternalError(w, err)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tif !allow {\n\t\t\twriteRateLimited(w)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Reserve, not just check: this books pageSize's worth of budget\n\t\t// against the window immediately, atomically with the capacity\n\t\t// check, so a second concurrent request from the same sub sees\n\t\t// this reservation and is correctly denied before either\n\t\t// request's DAO call returns — closing a check-then-act race a\n\t\t// separate Allowed()-then-RecordTouches() pair had, which could\n\t\t// let N concurrent requests each add a full page's worth of\n\t\t// touches before any of them recorded (Nolan Reyes, PR #26\n\t\t// review). Every exit past this point must call Settle exactly\n\t\t// once to release it.\n\t\treserved, err := d.TouchCounter.Reserve(ctx, ac.Sub, pageSize)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\twriteInternalError(w, err)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tif !reserved {\n\t\t\twriteTouchCapExceeded(w)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// The object-level policy hook, per v3: called after scope\n\t\t// validation (above) and request-shape validation (above), before\n\t\t// the DAO call. ScopeSearch has no single target id.\n\t\tallowed, err := d.Authz.Authorize(ctx, ac.Sub, ac.Scope, \"\")\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t_ = d.TouchCounter.Settle(ctx, ac.Sub, nil, pageSize)\n\t\t\twriteInternalError(w, err)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tif !allowed {\n\t\t\t_ = d.TouchCounter.Settle(ctx, ac.Sub, nil, pageSize)\n\t\t\twriteForbidden(w)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tq := dao.ProfileQuery{\n\t\t\tName:    req.Name,\n\t\t\tPhone:   req.Phone,\n\t\t\tRegion:  req.Region,\n\t\t\tCountry: req.Country,\n\t\t\tLimit:   pageSize,\n\t\t\tOffset:  offset,\n\t\t}\n\t\tresults, total, err := d.Repo.Profiles().Search(ctx, q)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t_ = d.TouchCounter.Settle(ctx, ac.Sub, nil, pageSize)\n\t\t\twriteDAOError(w, err)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tids := make([]string, len(results))\n\t\tmasked := make([]SearchResultProfile, len(results))\n\t\tfor i, p := range results {\n\t\t\tids[i] = p.ID\n\t\t\tmasked[i] = toSearchResult(p)\n\t\t}\n\t\tif err := d.TouchCounter.Settle(ctx, ac.Sub, ids, pageSize); err != nil {\n\t\t\twriteInternalError(w, err)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tresp := searchResponse{Results: masked, Total: total}\n\t\tif offset+len(results) < total {\n\t\t\tresp.NextCursor = encodeCursor(offset + len(results))\n\t\t}\n\t\twriteJSON(w, http.StatusOK, resp)\n\t}\n}\n\n// NewGetProfileHandler implements profile:read:own and profile:read:any\n// against the same handler — the two scopes differ in what Authorize()\n// checks (referral membership vs. a logged reason_code) and in whether\n// the cumulative touch cap applies at all. id is read from the request\n// path via r.PathValue(\"id\") (Go 1.22+ ServeMux pattern, e.g.\n// \"GET /profiles/{id}\"); this handler is not yet registered on any real\n// route (refinement/LT-39.md's Acceptance outcome — deferred until S7's\n// auth middleware exists to protect it), so tests call it directly with\n// http.Request.SetPathValue.\nfunc NewGetProfileHandler(d Deps) http.HandlerFunc {\n\treturn func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n\t\tctx := r.Context()\n\t\tac, ok := AuthContextFromContext(ctx)\n\t\tif !ok {\n\t\t\twriteUnauthorized(w)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tif ac.Scope != ScopeReadOwn && ac.Scope != ScopeReadAny {\n\t\t\twriteForbidden(w)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tid := r.PathValue(\"id\")\n\t\tif strings.TrimSpace(id) == \"\" {\n\t\t\twriteBadRequest(w, \"id is required\")\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// reasonCode is validated here and passed to Authorize() as part\n\t\t// of the audit trail this story doesn't build (refinement/\n\t\t// LT-39.md's non-goals) — there is deliberately no consumer of\n\t\t// the value itself beyond that validation. Threading it into an\n\t\t// actual audit record, and into Authorizer's signature if a real\n\t\t// implementation ever needs it for a policy decision rather than\n\t\t// only logging, is S7/a follow-up story's job, not this one's\n\t\t// (Oren Castellan, PR #26 review — flagging so this isn't\n\t\t// mistaken for wiring that already exists elsewhere).\n\t\tvar reasonCode ReasonCode\n\t\tif ac.Scope == ScopeReadAny {\n\t\t\treasonCode = ReasonCode(r.URL.Query().Get(\"reason_code\"))\n\t\t\tif !reasonCode.Valid() {\n\t\t\t\twriteBadRequest(w, \"reason_code is required for profile:read:any and must be one of the closed enum values\")\n\t\t\t\treturn\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tallow, err := d.RateLimiter.Allow(ctx, ac.Sub, ac.Scope, readRateLimit)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\twriteInternalError(w, err)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tif !allow {\n\t\t\twriteRateLimited(w)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// The cumulative cap applies to profile:search and\n\t\t// profile:read:any (handoff-03-auth.md v3, verbatim) — not\n\t\t// profile:read:own, which is bounded by the caller_referral\n\t\t// relationship rather than an open id space, so it isn't the\n\t\t// enumeration surface this control exists for. A valid, logged\n\t\t// reason_code on read:any satisfies Authorize() below but does\n\t\t// not exempt the caller from this cap — the exact regression v3\n\t\t// fixed (an earlier wording made this counter a no-op for\n\t\t// read:any, since that scope requires a reason_code on every call\n\t\t// by design).\n\t\tvar reserved bool\n\t\tif ac.Scope == ScopeReadAny {\n\t\t\treserved, err = d.TouchCounter.Reserve(ctx, ac.Sub, 1)\n\t\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t\twriteInternalError(w, err)\n\t\t\t\treturn\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif !reserved {\n\t\t\t\twriteTouchCapExceeded(w)\n\t\t\t\treturn\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tallowed, err := d.Authz.Authorize(ctx, ac.Sub, ac.Scope, id)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tif reserved {\n\t\t\t\t_ = d.TouchCounter.Settle(ctx, ac.Sub, nil, 1)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\twriteInternalError(w, err)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tif !allowed {\n\t\t\tif reserved {\n\t\t\t\t_ = d.TouchCounter.Settle(ctx, ac.Sub, nil, 1)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\twriteForbidden(w)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tp, err := d.Repo.Profiles().Get(ctx, id)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tif reserved {\n\t\t\t\t_ = d.TouchCounter.Settle(ctx, ac.Sub, nil, 1)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\twriteDAOError(w, err)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif reserved {\n\t\t\tif err := d.TouchCounter.Settle(ctx, ac.Sub, []string{p.ID}, 1); err != nil {\n\t\t\t\twriteInternalError(w, err)\n\t\t\t\treturn\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\n\t\twriteJSON(w, http.StatusOK, toProfileResponse(p))\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc writeJSON(w http.ResponseWriter, status int, body any) {\n\tw.Header().Set(\"Content-Type\", \"application/json\")\n\tw.WriteHeader(status)\n\t_ = json.NewEncoder(w).Encode(body)\n}\n"

{
  "file_path": "/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-worktrees/LT-39/internal/api/audit.go",
  "content": "package api\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"encoding/json\"\n\t\"log\"\n\t\"time\"\n)\n\n// AuditEventKind names a policy decision for the audit trail —\n// handoff-03-auth.md v3's audit-log field list requires \"policy decision\n// (allow/deny, and if deny, whether it was a scope failure or an\n// authorize() policy denial — these are distinct signals for detection)\"\n// on every search/retrieve call. The client-visible response body is\n// deliberately identical for AuditScopeFailure and AuditPolicyDenial\n// (decisions/error-semantics.md F7) — this is the ONLY place that\n// distinction is allowed to exist.\ntype AuditEventKind string\n\nconst (\n\tAuditAuthnFailure  AuditEventKind = \"authn_failure\"\n\tAuditScopeFailure  AuditEventKind = \"scope_failure\"\n\tAuditPolicyDenial  AuditEventKind = \"authorize_denial\"\n\tAuditRateLimited   AuditEventKind = \"rate_limited\"\n\tAuditCapExceeded   AuditEventKind = \"cumulative_cap_exceeded\"\n\tAuditRequestFailed AuditEventKind = \"request_failed\" // a DAO/internal error, not a policy decision\n\tAuditSuccess       AuditEventKind = \"success\"\n)\n\n// AuditEvent carries exactly the fields handoff-03-auth.md v3's audit-log\n// section names, and only those — RecordIDs are opaque ids, never PII\n// field values; ReasonCode is empty unless the request was\n// ScopeReadAny. Never add a field here without checking\n// handoff-04-secrets.md's never-log list first (see AuditEvent's own\n// logger implementations for the enforcement site).\ntype AuditEvent struct {\n\tSub        string\n\tScope      Scope\n\tKind       AuditEventKind\n\tRecordIDs  []string\n\tReasonCode ReasonCode\n\tTimestamp  time.Time\n}\n\n// AuditLogger is the seam this story wires (Ingrid Solano's PR #26\n// review, ruled by the PM as an unmet acceptance criterion, not a\n// follow-up story): handoff-03-auth.md v3 requires reason_code and the\n// scope/authorize() distinction to be \"logged with the audit record\" —\n// this interface is what every handler decision point calls, regardless\n// of which real sink backs it. A structured-stdout implementation is\n// sufficient for this story; 04's retention/RBAC-separated log pipeline\n// is a separate, infrastructure-side concern this interface doesn't\n// depend on.\ntype AuditLogger interface {\n\tLog(ctx context.Context, event AuditEvent)\n}\n\n// StdoutAuditLogger writes one JSON line per event to the process's\n// standard logger. Real production wiring (a shipped log pipeline with\n// the RBAC separation handoff-04-secrets.md requires between Secret\n// access and audit-log access) is 04's infrastructure work; this\n// satisfies \"events are actually emitted somewhere real\" for this story\n// without waiting on that pipeline to exist.\ntype StdoutAuditLogger struct{}\n\nfunc (StdoutAuditLogger) Log(_ context.Context, event AuditEvent) {\n\tif event.Timestamp.IsZero() {\n\t\tevent.Timestamp = time.Now().UTC()\n\t}\n\tb, err := json.Marshal(event)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tlog.Printf(\"api: audit event marshal error: %v\", err)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tlog.Printf(\"audit: %s\", b)\n}\n"
}

ctx.sub,\n\t\tScope:      ctx.scope,\n\t\tKind:       kind,\n\t\tRecordIDs:  recordIDs,\n\t\tReasonCode: reasonCode,\n\t\tTimestamp:  time.Now().UTC(),\n\t})\n}\n\n// AuditCtx is the small bundle either limiter, both limiters\n// before authorize()) rather than the two handlers disagreeing on it for\n// no stated reason (Oren Castellan, PR #26 review): auth context present\n// -> scope is ScopeSearch -> request shape (unknown fields, empty body,\n// page_size cap) -> per-minute rate limit -> cumulative touch cap ->\n// authorize() -> the DAO call -> settle touches -> masked response. Scope\n// mismatch and an authorize() denial return an identical client-visible\n// body (F7); the audit log below is the only place that distinction is\n// allowed to exist (Ingrid Solano, PR #26 review — this story's\n// acceptance criteria require the distinction to be logged, not merely\n// kept out of the response).\nfunc NewSearchHandler(d Deps) http.HandlerFunc {\n\treturn func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n\t\tctx := r.Context()\n\t\tac, ok := AuthContextFromContext(ctx)\n\t\tif !ok {\n\t\t\td.audit(AuditCtx{ctx: ctx}, AuditAuthnFailure, nil, \"\")\n\t\t\twriteUnauthorized(w)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tactx := AuditCtx{ctx: ctx, sub: ac.Sub, scope: ac.Scope}\n\t\tif ac.Scope != ScopeSearch {\n\t\t\td.audit(actx, AuditScopeFailure, nil, \"\")\n\t\t\twriteForbidden(w)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tvar req searchRequest\n\t\tif r.Body != nil {\n\t\t\tdec := json.NewDecoder(r.Body)\n\t\t\t// Reject any field this shape doesn't name — in particular\n\t\t\t// an \"offset\" key, which would otherwise be silently\n\t\t\t// ignored rather than rejected. The API-facing contract is\n\t\t\t// cursor-only pagination (handoff-03-auth.md v3's F-pag);\n\t\t\t// a caller-supplied offset-shaped parameter is exactly the\n\t\t\t// bypass that contract exists to close, so it must fail\n\t\t\t// loudly, not be dropped quietly (Ingrid Solano, PR #26\n\t\t\t// review).\n\t\t\tdec.DisallowUnknownFields()\n\t\t\tif err := dec.Decode(&req); err != nil {\n\t\t\t\twriteBadRequest(w, \"malformed request body or unrecognized field\")\n\t\t\t\treturn\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// A search request with no filter field at all is rejected here\n\t\t// with a 400 before it ever reaches the DAO — the DAO's own\n\t\t// all-nil ProfileQuery is legal and returns every profile, which\n\t\t// this API must not expose directly (handoff-03-auth.md v3, F10).\n\t\tif req.Name == nil && req.Phone == nil && req.Region == nil && req.Country == nil {\n\t\t\twriteBadRequest(w, \"at least one search filter is required\")\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\toffset, err := decodeCursor(req.Cursor)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\twriteBadRequest(w, \"malformed cursor\")\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tpageSize := req.PageSize\n\t\tif pageSize <= 0 {\n\t\t\tpageSize = defaultPageSize\n\t\t}\n\t\tif pageSize > maxPageSize {\n\t\t\t// A bare/unconstrained request for more than this API's\n\t\t\t// result cap is rejected outright, not truncated\n\t\t\t// (handoff-03-auth.md v3) — checked here, before the query\n\t\t\t// is built, mirroring the same rule for authorize() below.\n\t\t\twriteBadRequest(w, \"page_size exceeds the maximum of 50\")\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tallow, err := d.RateLimiter.Allow(ctx, ac.Sub, ScopeSearch, searchRateLimit)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\twriteInternalError(w, err)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tif !allow {\n\t\t\td.audit(actx, AuditRateLimited, nil, \"\")\n\t\t\twriteRateLimited(w)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Reserve, not just check: this books pageSize's worth of budget\n\t\t// against the window immediately, atomically with the capacity\n\t\t// check, so a second concurrent request from the same sub sees\n\t\t// this reservation and is correctly denied before either\n\t\t// request's DAO call returns — closing a check-then-act race a\n\t\t// separate Allowed()-then-RecordTouches() pair had, which could\n\t\t// let N concurrent requests each add a full page's worth of\n\t\t// touches before any of them recorded (Nolan Reyes, PR #26\n\t\t// review). Every exit past this point must call Settle exactly\n\t\t// once to release it.\n\t\treserved, err := d.TouchCounter.Reserve(ctx, ac.Sub, pageSize)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\twriteInternalError(w, err)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tif !reserved {\n\t\t\td.audit(actx, AuditCapExceeded, nil, \"\")\n\t\t\twriteTouchCapExceeded(w)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// The object-level policy hook, per v3: called after scope\n\t\t// validation (above) and request-shape validation (above), before\n\t\t// the DAO call. ScopeSearch has no single target id.\n\t\tallowed, err := d.Authz.Authorize(ctx, ac.Sub, ac.Scope, \"\")\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t_ = d.TouchCounter.Settle(ctx, ac.Sub, nil, pageSize)\n\t\t\twriteInternalError(w, err)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tif !allowed {\n\t\t\t_ = d.TouchCounter.Settle(ctx, ac.Sub, nil, pageSize)\n\t\t\td.audit(actx, AuditPolicyDenial, nil, \"\")\n\t\t\twriteForbidden(w)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tq := dao.ProfileQuery{\n\t\t\tName:    req.Name,\n\t\t\tPhone:   req.Phone,\n\t\t\tRegion:  req.Region,\n\t\t\tCountry: req.Country,\n\t\t\tLimit:   pageSize,\n\t\t\tOffset:  offset,\n\t\t}\n\t\tresults, total, err := d.Repo.Profiles().Search(ctx, q)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t_ = d.TouchCounter.Settle(ctx, ac.Sub, nil, pageSize)\n\t\t\td.audit(actx, AuditRequestFailed, nil, \"\")\n\t\t\twriteDAOError(w, err)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tids := make([]string, len(results))\n\t\tmasked := make([]SearchResultProfile, len(results))\n\t\tfor i, p := range results {\n\t\t\tids[i] = p.ID\n\t\t\tmasked[i] = toSearchResult(p)\n\t\t}\n\t\tif err := d.TouchCounter.Settle(ctx, ac.Sub, ids, pageSize); err != nil {\n\t\t\twriteInternalError(w, err)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\td.audit(actx, AuditSuccess, ids, \"\")\n\t\tresp := searchResponse{Results: masked, Total: total}\n\t\tif offset+len(results) < total {\n\t\t\tresp.NextCursor = encodeCursor(offset + len(results))\n\t\t}\n\t\twriteJSON(w, http.StatusOK, resp)\n\t}\n}\n\n// NewGetProfileHandler implements profile:read:own and profile:read:any\n// against the same handler — the two scopes differ in what Authorize()\n// checks (referral membership vs. a logged reason_code) and in whether\n// the cumulative touch cap applies at all. id is read from the request\n// path via r.PathValue(\"id\") (Go 1.22+ ServeMux pattern, e.g.\n// \"GET /profiles/{id}\"); this handler is not yet registered on any real\n// route (refinement/LT-39.md's Acceptance outcome — deferred until S7's\n// auth middleware exists to protect it), so tests call it directly with\n// http.Request.SetPathValue.\nfunc NewGetProfileHandler(d Deps) http.HandlerFunc {\n\treturn func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n\t\tctx := r.Context()\n\t\tac, ok := AuthContextFromContext(ctx)\n\t\tif !ok {\n\t\t\td.audit(AuditCtx{ctx: ctx}, AuditAuthnFailure, nil, \"\")\n\t\t\twriteUnauthorized(w)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tactx := AuditCtx{ctx: ctx, sub: ac.Sub, scope: ac.Scope}\n\t\tif ac.Scope != ScopeReadOwn && ac.Scope != ScopeReadAny {\n\t\t\td.audit(actx, AuditScopeFailure, nil, \"\")\n\t\t\twriteForbidden(w)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tid := r.PathValue(\"id\")\n\t\tif strings.TrimSpace(id) == \"\" {\n\t\t\twriteBadRequest(w, \"id is required\")\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// reasonCode is validated here and logged with every audit event\n\t\t// on this request (handoff-03-auth.md v3: \"reason_code, when the\n\t\t// scope is profile:read:any\" is a required audit-log field).\n\t\t// There is deliberately no other consumer of the value — no\n\t\t// policy decision in this story reads it beyond validating its\n\t\t// membership in the closed enum; threading it into Authorizer's\n\t\t// signature is a follow-up story's job if a real policy\n\t\t// implementation ever needs it for more than logging (Oren\n\t\t// Castellan, PR #26 review).\n\t\tvar reasonCode ReasonCode\n\t\tif ac.Scope == ScopeReadAny {\n\t\t\treasonCode = ReasonCode(r.URL.Query().Get(\"reason_code\"))\n\t\t\tif !reasonCode.Valid() {\n\t\t\t\twriteBadRequest(w, \"reason_code is required for profile:read:any and must be one of the closed enum values\")\n\t\t\t\treturn\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tallow, err := d.RateLimiter.Allow(ctx, ac.Sub, ac.Scope, readRateLimit)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\twriteInternalError(w, err)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tif !allow {\n\t\t\td.audit(actx, AuditRateLimited, nil, reasonCode)\n\t\t\twriteRateLimited(w)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// The cumulative cap applies to profile:search and\n\t\t// profile:read:any (handoff-03-auth.md v3, verbatim) — not\n\t\t// profile:read:own, which is bounded by the caller_referral\n\t\t// relationship rather than an open id space, so it isn't the\n\t\t// enumeration surface this control exists for. A valid, logged\n\t\t// reason_code on read:any satisfies Authorize() below but does\n\t\t// not exempt the caller from this cap — the exact regression v3\n\t\t// fixed (an earlier wording made this counter a no-op for\n\t\t// read:any, since that scope requires a reason_code on every call\n\t\t// by design).\n\t\tvar reserved bool\n\t\tif ac.Scope == ScopeReadAny {\n\t\t\treserved, err = d.TouchCounter.Reserve(ctx, ac.Sub, 1)\n\t\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t\twriteInternalError(w, err)\n\t\t\t\treturn\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif !reserved {\n\t\t\t\td.audit(actx, AuditCapExceeded, nil, reasonCode)\n\t\t\t\twriteTouchCapExceeded(w)\n\t\t\t\treturn\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tallowed, err := d.Authz.Authorize(ctx, ac.Sub, ac.Scope, id)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tif reserved {\n\t\t\t\t_ = d.TouchCounter.Settle(ctx, ac.Sub, nil, 1)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\twriteInternalError(w, err)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tif !allowed {\n\t\t\tif reserved {\n\t\t\t\t_ = d.TouchCounter.Settle(ctx, ac.Sub, nil, 1)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\td.audit(actx, AuditPolicyDenial, []string{id}, reasonCode)\n\t\t\twriteForbidden(w)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tp, err := d.Repo.Profiles().Get(ctx, id)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tif reserved {\n\t\t\t\t_ = d.TouchCounter.Settle(ctx, ac.Sub, nil, 1)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\td.audit(actx, AuditRequestFailed, []string{id}, reasonCode)\n\t\t\twriteDAOError(w, err)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif reserved {\n\t\t\tif err := d.TouchCounter.Settle(ctx, ac.Sub, []string{p.ID}, 1); err != nil {\n\t\t\t\twriteInternalError(w, err)\n\t\t\t\treturn\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\n\t\td.audit(actx, AuditSuccess, []string{p.ID}, reasonCode)\n\t\twriteJSON(w, http.StatusOK, toProfileResponse(p))\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc writeJSON(w http.ResponseWriter, status int, body any) {\n\tw.Header().Set(\"Content-Type\", \"application/json\")\n\tw.WriteHeader(status)\n\t_ = json.NewEncoder(w).Encode(body)\n}\n\nvar _ = bytes.NewReader // silence unused import if bytes ends up unused after edits; removed below if truly unnecessary\n"

13	// Deps bundles a handler's collaborators — the DAO, the object-level
14	// policy hook, the two independent limiters, and the audit sink — so
15	// NewSearchHandler/NewGetProfileHandler take one argument instead of
16	// five, and a test fake for any one of them doesn't require constructing
17	// the other four by hand each time.
18	type Deps struct {
19		Repo         dao.Repository
20		Authz        Authorizer
21		RateLimiter  RateLimiter
22		TouchCounter TouchCounter
23		AuditLog     AuditLogger
24	}
25	
26	const (
27		defaultPageSize = 20
28		maxPageSize     = 50
29		readRateLimit   = 60 // req/min, read:own and read:any
30		searchRateLimit = 10 // req/min, profile:search
31	)
32	
33	type searchRequest struct {
34		Name     *string `json:"name,omitempty"`
35		Phone    *string `json:"phone,omitempty"`
36		Region   *string `json:"region,omitempty"`
37		Country  *string `json:"country,omitempty"`
38		Cursor   string  `json:"cursor,omitempty"`
39		PageSize int     `json:"page_size,omitempty"`
40	}
41	
42	type searchResponse struct {
43		Results    []SearchResultProfile `json:"results"`
44		Total      int                   `json:"total"`
45		NextCursor string                `json:"next_cursor,omitempty"`
46	}
47	
48	func (d Deps) audit(ctx AuditCtx, kind AuditEventKind, recordIDs []string, reasonCode ReasonCode) {
49		d.AuditLog.Log(ctx.ctx, AuditEvent{
50			Sub:        ctx.sub,
51			Scope:      ctx.scope,
52			Kind:       kind,
53			RecordIDs:  recordIDs,
54			ReasonCode: reasonCode,
55			Timestamp:  time.Now().UTC(),
56		})
57	}
58	
59	// AuditCtx is the small bundle handler code passes to Deps.audit — a
60	// context.Context plus the sub/scope an audit event needs, since those
61	// two are read from AuthContext once per request rather than threaded as
62	// separate parameters to every call.
63	type AuditCtx struct {
64		ctx   any // context.Context, typed any only to avoid an import cycle concern that doesn't exist — see note below
65		sub   string
66		scope Scope
67	}
68

11	// Hand-written fakes, per decisions/test-double-strategy.md: any change
12	// to Repository or its three sub-interfaces must update the corresponding
13	// fake in the same PR — a review-checklist item, not codegen.

15	// fakeRepository implements dao.Repository. Only Profiles() is exercised
16	// by this package's handlers; the rest panic if ever called, so a test
17	// that reaches them fails loudly instead of silently zero-valuing.
18	type fakeRepository struct {
19		profiles fakeProfileRepository
20	}
21	
22	func (f *fakeRepository) Profiles() dao.ProfileRepository       { return &f.profiles }
23	func (f *fakeRepository) Credentials() dao.CredentialRepository { panic("not used by internal/api") }
24	func (f *fakeRepository) Methods() dao.AuthMethodRepository     { panic("not used by internal/api") }

26		panic("not used by internal/api")
27	}
28	func (f *fakeRepository) DeleteExpired(ctx context.Context, class dao.RetentionClass, olderThan time.Time, maxRows int) (dao.SweepResult, error) {
29		panic("not used by internal/api")
30	}
31	func (f *fakeRepository) DeleteProfile(ctx context.Context, id string, externalRef *string) error {
32		panic("not used by internal/api")
33	}
34	func (f *fakeRepository) Close() error { return nil }

36	// fakeProfileRepository implements dao.ProfileRepository with
37	// configurable funcs per test, per the ruled test-double strategy.
38	type fakeProfileRepository struct {
39		getFn    func(ctx context.Context, id string) (*model.UserProfile, error)
40		searchFn func(ctx context.Context, q dao.ProfileQuery) ([]model.UserProfile, int, error)
41	}

45	}
46	func (f *fakeProfileRepository) Search(ctx context.Context, q dao.ProfileQuery) ([]model.UserProfile, int, error) {
47		return f.searchFn(ctx, q)
48	}

50		panic("not used by internal/api")

66	}

70	}
71	
72	// fakeRateLimiter implements RateLimiter with a configurable decision —
73	// tests exercising the rate-limit path don't need real wall-clock timing.
74	type fakeRateLimiter struct {
75		allow bool
76		err   error
77	}
78	
79	func (f *fakeRateLimiter) Allow(ctx context.Context, sub string, scope Scope, limit int) (bool, error) {
80		return f.allow, f.err
81	}
82	
83	// fakeTouchCounter implements TouchCounter with configurable behavior.
84	type fakeTouchCounter struct {
85		allowed     bool
86		allowedErr  error
87		recordFn    func(ctx context.Context, sub string, recordIDs []string) (int, error)
88		recordedIDs []string
89	}
90	
91	func (f *fakeTouchCounter) Allowed(ctx context.Context, sub string) (bool, error) {
92		return f.allowed, f.allowedErr
93	}
94	func (f *fakeTouchCounter) RecordTouches(ctx context.Context, sub string, recordIDs []string) (int, error) {
95		f.recordedIDs = append(f.recordedIDs, recordIDs...)
96		if f.recordFn != nil {
97			return f.recordFn(ctx, sub, recordIDs)
98		}
99		return len(f.recordedIDs), nil
100	}
101	
102	// allowAllDeps builds a Deps whose collaborators all say yes — the
103	// baseline for a test that then overrides exactly one collaborator to
104	// exercise its own denial path.
105	func allowAllDeps() Deps {
106		return Deps{
107			Repo:         &fakeRepository{},
108			Authz:        &fakeAuthorizer{allow: true},
109			RateLimiter:  &fakeRateLimiter{allow: true},
110			TouchCounter: &fakeTouchCounter{allowed: true},
111		}

24*time.Hour)\n\tctx := context.Background()\n\n\tif _, err := c.Reserve(ctx, \"clientA\", 3); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"Reserve: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tif err := c.Settle(ctx, \"clientA\", []string{\"r1\", \"r2\", \"r1\"}, 3); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"Settle: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\t// A 4th distinct touch should now be denied: r1+r2 = 2 distinct, cap\n\t// is 3, so exactly one more distinct id fits.\n\tallowed, err := c.Reserve(ctx, \"clientA\", 2)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"Reserve: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tif !allowed {\n\t\tt.Fatal(\"should still have budget for 1 more distinct touch (2 used of 3)\")\n\t}\n\tif err := c.Settle(ctx, \"clientA\", []string{\"r3\", \"r4\"}, 2); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"Settle: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\t// Duplicates (r1, r2) must not have inflated the count: after settling\n\t// [r1,r2,r1] then [r3,r4], the distinct set is {r1,r2,r3,r4} = 4,\n\t// which is already over the cap of 3 — the next Reserve must deny.\n\tallowed, err = c.Reserve(ctx, \"clientA\", 1)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"Reserve: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tif allowed {\n\t\tt.Error(\"should be denied once distinct touches reach the cap\")\n\t}\n}\n\n// TestInProcessTouchCounter_ReserveBooksBudgetBeforeSettle is the core\n// concurrency fix (Nolan Reyes, PR #26 review): a second Reserve must see\n// the first's booked budget even before the first has called Settle —\n// otherwise concurrent requests can each pass Reserve and together\n// overshoot the cap by a multiple of their own page size.\nfunc TestInProcessTouchCounter_ReserveBooksBudgetBeforeSettle(t *testing.T) {\n\tc := NewInProcessTouchCounter(10, 24*time.Hour)\n\tctx := context.Background()\n\n\t// First request reserves its full page size (10) — books the whole\n\t// cap immediately, before it has queried anything or called Settle.\n\tok1, err := c.Reserve(ctx, \"clientA\", 10)\n\tif err != nil || !ok1 {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"first Reserve: ok=%v err=%v, want true/nil\", ok1, err)\n\t}\n\n\t// A second concurrent request's Reserve must now be denied — not\n\t// because any touches were recorded yet, but because the first\n\t// request's reservation already consumes the whole window's budget.\n\tok2, err := c.Reserve(ctx, \"clientB2\", 10) // different sub: must NOT be denied\n\tif err != nil || !ok2 {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"a different sub's Reserve must not be affected by clientA's reservation: ok=%v err=%v\", ok2, err)\n\t}\n\tok3, err := c.Reserve(ctx, \"clientA\", 1)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"Reserve: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tif ok3 {\n\t\tt.Error(\"a second concurrent Reserve for the SAME sub must be denied while the first reservation is still outstanding\")\n\t}\n}\n\n// TestInProcessTouchCounter_SettleReleasesUnusedReservation covers the\n// common case: a page returns fewer distinct rows than its reserved\n// page size, and the difference must be released, not permanently\n// consumed.\nfunc TestInProcessTouchCounter_SettleReleasesUnusedReservation(t *testing.T) {\n\tc := NewInProcessTouchCounter(5, 24*time.Hour)\n\tctx := context.Background()\n\n\tif _, err := c.Reserve(ctx, \"clientA\", 5); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"Reserve: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\t// Only 2 of the reserved 5 were actually touched (a short page, or a\n\t// denied/failed request settling with recordIDs=nil).\n\tif err := c.Settle(ctx, \"clientA\", []string{\"r1\", \"r2\"}, 5); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"Settle: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\t// The unused 3 must be released: a new request should be able to\n\t// reserve up to 3 more before hitting the cap of 5.\n\tok, err := c.Reserve(ctx, \"clientA\", 3)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"Reserve: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tif !ok {\n\t\tt.Error(\"unused reservation budget must be released by Settle, not held forever\")\n\t}\n}\n\n// TestInProcessTouchCounter_HardDeniesAfterCrossingCap is\n// handoff-03-auth.md v3's ruling: crossing the cap hard-denies further\n// requests for the remainder of the window — the request that crosses\n// the cap itself still completes (it already held a valid reservation),\n// but the next one is denied.\nfunc TestInProcessTouchCounter_HardDeniesAfterCrossingCap(t *testing.T) {\n\tc := NewInProcessTouchCounter(2, 24*time.Hour)\n\tctx := context.Background()\n\n\tok, err := c.Reserve(ctx, \"clientA\", 3) // reserving more than the cap is itself allowed once\n\tif err != nil || !ok {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"first Reserve: ok=%v err=%v, want true/nil\", ok, err)\n\t}\n\tif err := c.Settle(ctx, \"clientA\", []string{\"r1\", \"r2\", \"r3\"}, 3); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"Settle: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\t// This request's own 3 touches pushed the distinct count to 3, over\n\t// the cap of 2 — but it had already been allowed to proceed.\n\tallowed, err := c.Reserve(ctx, \"clientA\", 1)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"Reserve: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tif allowed {\n\t\tt.Error(\"a subsequent Reserve after crossing the cap must return false\")\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestInProcessTouchCounter_WindowResets(t *testing.T) {\n\tc := NewInProcessTouchCounter(1, time.Hour)\n\tfakeNow := time.Now()\n\tc.now = func() time.Time { return fakeNow }\n\tctx := context.Background()\n\n\tif _, err := c.Reserve(ctx, \"clientA\", 1); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"Reserve: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tif err := c.Settle(ctx, \"clientA\", []string{\"r1\"}, 1); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"Settle: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tif allowed, _ := c.Reserve(ctx, \"clientA\", 1); allowed {\n\t\tt.Fatal(\"should be denied at the cap within the same window\")\n\t}\n\n\tfakeNow = fakeNow.Add(time.Hour + time.Second)\n\tif allowed, _ := c.Reserve(ctx, \"clientA\", 1); !allowed {\n\t\tt.Error(\"should be allowed again once the rolling window has passed\")\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestInProcessTouchCounter_KeyedPerSub(t *testing.T) {\n\tc := NewInProcessTouchCounter(1, 24*time.Hour)\n\tctx := context.Background()\n\tif err)\n\t}\n\tallowed, err := c.Reserve(ctx, \"clientB\", 1)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"Reserve: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tif !allowed {\n\t\tt.Error(\"a different sub must have its own independent evicted from the map, not held forever.\nfunc TestInProcessTouchCounter_SweepEvictsStaleWindows(t *testing.T) {\n\tc := NewInProcessTouchCounter(10, time.Hour)\n\tfakeNow := time.Now()\n\tc.now = func() time.Time { return fakeNow }\n\tctx := context.Background()\n\n\tif _, err := c.Reserve(ctx, \"clientA\", 1); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"Reserve: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tif err := c.Settle(ctx, \"clientA\", []string{\"r1\"}, 1); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"Settle: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tif _, ok := c.windows[\"clientA\"]; !ok {\n\t\tt.Fatal(\"expected an entry for clientA before any // reserving a full page's worth of budget, and asserts the total ever
// granted never exceeds the cap. This is the actual failure mode Nolan
// Reyes's review described: N concurrent requests each reading
// "allowed" before any of them recorded a touch. Run with -race.
func TestInProcessTouchCounter_ConcurrentReservesNeverExceedCap(t *testing.T) {
	const cap = 100
	const pageSize = 50
	const concurrentRequests = 20 // 20*50 = 1000, 10x the cap if the race isn't closed

	c := NewInProcessTouchCounter(cap, 24*time.Hour)
	ctx := context.Background()

	var wg sync.WaitGroup
	var mu sync.Mutex
	granted := 0
	for i := 0; i < concurrentRequests; i++ {
		wg.Add(1)
		go func() {
			defer wg.Done()
			ok, err := c.Reserve(ctx, "clientA", pageSize)
			if err != nil {
				t.Errorf("Reserve: %v", err)
				return
			}
			if ok {
				mu.Lock()
				granted++
				mu.Unlock()
			}
		}()
	}
	wg.Wait()

Under\n  concurrent requests from the same sub, every one of them could read\n  Allowed()==true before any recorded a touch, letting N concurrent\n  requests each add a full page's worth of distinct touches — overshoot\n  by a multiple of the page size, not the one-request overshoot the\n  design accepts. 153:- [ ] The issued JWT's claims (`iss`, `aud`, `scope`, `exp`, `sub`) and algorithm/`kid` header match what LT-40's verifier middleware expects and validates — enforcement site: an integration test that mints a token here and verifies it through LT-40's actual middleware, not two independently-trusted unit tests that never touch each other.

156:- [ ] This story's own failed-grant rate limiting/backoff (LT-40's criterion 5) applies to this endpoint's real implementation, not just the placeholder — confirmed by re-running LT-40's rate-limit tests against the real grant logic, not assumed to still hold.

18	
19	All three accepted; no decline.
20	
21	1. Any `Repository` method that writes to more than one table (e.g., a profile+credential save) runs inside a single `Tx`. Context cancellation during that transaction triggers `Tx.Rollback`, never a partial commit — this is a stated requirement on the `Repository` implementation contract, not left to each backend's discretion.
22	2. Deadline propagation is a budget-allocation decision, stated explicitly in S3's middleware design: the HTTP-boundary timeout must be sized to cover realistic p99 DAO latency *plus* any upstream work (e.g., no DAO call should ever be handed a near-zero remaining deadline as a matter of routine operation). This gets a concrete number attached when S3's middleware design is written, not left as "some deadline."
23	3. Credential writes are named explicitly as the path that needs a detach point for durability, distinct from profile reads and other cancel-safe paths, which propagate the inbound context unmodified. This distinction is documented at the point each such method is implemented, not left implicit.
24	
25	## Status
26	
27	Ruled. Carries into S2 (Repository implementation contract: transaction scoping, detach points) and S3 (middleware deadline budget).
28	
29	## Resolved against 05's contract (2026-09-12)
30	
31	Oren's first-pass review of 05's DAO contract flagged a real tension: this ruling's item 1 requires any multi-table write to run in a single `Tx`, but the contract as first landed had no method spanning both `user_profile` and `user_credential`, and explicitly barred 03 from composing a cross-call transaction — leaving registration's profile+credential creation with no atomic path at all. 05 resolved it by adding `CreateProfileWithCredential(ctx, p, c)` on the `Repository` composite (05's §3b) — the one cross-table operation in the contract, DAO-generated IDs for both rows, single transaction, `c.UserID` ignored on input and set from the just-created profile. This ruling's item 1 now has exactly one caller-visible method satisfying it, rather than being a requirement with no concrete instance.
