1	# LT-40 refinement — S7: auth middleware (JWT validation)
2	
3	**Product Owner:** Naomi Voss (01). **Research team:** Imogen Hale, Desmond Okafor, Tobias Lindqvist. **Engineering:** Renata Cole (03). **Source:** `03-engineering-delivery/backlog.md` S7 (LT-40); `02-ai-security-architecture/api-auth-design.md`, `handoff-03-auth.md` v4 (v3 unchanged except where v4 states otherwise).
4	
5	**Who did the work:** Refined by Naomi Voss (PO); claims check Desmond Okafor; objection Tobias Lindqvist; engineering counterpart Renata Cole.
6	
7	## Why this one, now, and at priority
8	
9	This is the critical path per team-lead's direction: LT-39's handlers are code-review-passed but deliberately unwired (exposing them before real auth exists would serve unauthenticated PII), LT-32's issuer split is deployed and live at `/auth/token` (currently a 501 placeholder), and LT-34's live-URL story stays open until something real is reachable through auth. This story is what turns all of that into an actual, reviewable, authenticated public endpoint.
10	
11	## Correction to `backlog.md`'s own story text, found before drafting
12	
13	**S7's acceptance criterion 6 ("per-client-credential rate limits and the cumulative distinct-record-touch counter ... enforcement site: the rate-limit middleware plus its own test") is stale relative to work already merged.** `backlog.md` was written before LT-39 (PR #26) implemented `RateLimiter` and `TouchCounter` as explicit, tested checks called *inside* each handler (via a `Deps` struct), not as separate HTTP middleware — confirmed by reading `internal/api/auth.go`, `handlers.go`, `ratelimit.go`, `touchcounter.go` directly. Both are already built, both are already tested (including the two hard-deny regression tests from LT-39's own refinement), and both are already wired to whatever `AuthContext` the request context carries.
14	
15	**What this means for LT-40's actual scope:** this story does **not** rebuild rate limiting or the touch counter. Its job is narrower and already well-seamed: verify the JWT, populate `AuthContext` (via `WithAuthContext`, already exported by `internal/api`) with the token's `sub` and `scope` claims, and wire the resulting middleware into `cmd/api-service`'s router ahead of the already-built handlers. The 90-day-re-approval/lifecycle-revocation machinery (`api-auth-design.md`) and the issuer-side rate limiting (criterion 5, below) are the only genuinely new rate-limit-shaped work this story adds — the resource-API limits are already done.
16	
17	## Acceptance outcome
18	
19	"Done" for this story is a working, tested JWT-verification middleware wired into `cmd/api-service`'s router, with LT-39's handlers reachable through it at the live URL. **Corrected per team-lead, per Marcus's actual design:** nothing ever signs with the deployed private key outside the issuer process — there is no "out-of-band token minted directly against the signing key" path, review or otherwise; the private key's only caller is `/auth/token`'s own real grant logic (LT-51). **LT-40 merges and deploys to In Test on its own** (middleware + wiring, verified by unit tests here), **but its live-URL review happens together with LT-51's**, using a real token obtained from `/auth/token` via a dedicated, narrowly-scoped QA client credential seeded in the client store (LT-51's own criteria). LT-40 and LT-51 go Done together, in one PO pass, with a genuinely issued token — not LT-40 alone on a workaround token.
20	
21	## Acceptance criteria (restated from `backlog.md` S7, corrected per the finding above)
22	
23	- [ ] Implements 02's token scheme without re-deriving the no-refresh/no-denylist reasoning in `api-auth-design.md` — no refresh tokens (client re-authenticates on expiry); TTL-only revocation (5–15 min), no denylist, an explicitly accepted trade-off, not to be re-litigated.
24	- [ ] Rejects any token whose `aud` doesn't match this service's audience — enforcement site: the claim-validation step, unit test with a mismatched-audience token.
25	- [ ] Verifies with the public key only; the private signing key never touches this binary's code path — enforcement site: dependency/import check, no signing-key material referenced anywhere in `internal/api` or the verifier's own binary path (this is already structurally true per LT-32's Deployment split; this criterion confirms the *code*, not just the *Deployment*, never imports signing-key material).
26	- [ ] **Pins the expected signing algorithm and rejects any token whose header claims a different one**, including `alg: none` and a symmetric-algorithm downgrade using the public key as an HMAC secret (RFC 8725 §3.1) — enforcement site: an explicit algorithm allowlist check in the JWT verification call, never left to the library's default. Unit tests: a token with `alg: none`, a token HMAC-signed using the public key bytes as the secret, both rejected.
27	- [ ] `kid`-based key rotation: the middleware selects the verification key by the token's `kid` header claim, supporting at least two concurrently valid public keys during a rotation overlap window — enforcement site: a unit test presenting tokens signed under two different `kid`s, both verifying successfully when both keys are configured.
28	- [ ] The middleware populates `internal/api.AuthContext` (via the already-exported `WithAuthContext`) with the token's `sub` and `scope` claims on success, and returns 401 (via LT-39's existing unauthorized-response path, not a new one) on any verification failure — **this is the actual integration seam with S6's work, not new response-shape design.**
29	- [ ] The token-issuing endpoint (`APP_MODE=issuer`, currently the 501 placeholder from LT-32) gets its own failed-grant rate limiting and progressive backoff per `client_id` and per source IP, separate from the resource-API limits (which are already built in `internal/api`) — with alerting on a sustained run of failed grants against one `client_id`. This is genuinely new work, not already covered by LT-39.
30	- [ ] The deadline budget for a request (context propagation into the DAO layer) is sized to cover realistic p99 DAO latency per `decisions/context-propagation.md`'s ruling — stated as a concrete number in this story's implementation, not left as "some deadline."
31	- [ ] The resource-API rate limiter and touch counter already built in LT-39 are wired to real, JWT-derived `AuthContext.Sub` values once this middleware populates them — **no new implementation, an integration check**: a live-URL test with a real token confirms the 60/10 req/min limits and the touch cap actually apply per real client identity, not per a test fake's hardcoded value.
32	
33	## Non-goals
34	
35	- **Real token issuance logic** (actually minting a signed JWT from valid client credentials at `/auth/token`) is explicitly out of scope for this story unless `backlog.md`/02 says otherwise — LT-32 only built the placeholder route and the signing-key custody; this story is the *verifier* side. Flagged as an open question below, since without it there's no way to obtain a real token for the live-URL review other than an out-of-band test token minted directly against the deployed key.
36	- No change to `internal/api`'s handler logic, masking, or error translation — that's LT-39 (PR #26, code-review-passed, not yet merged to main).
37	- No change to the object-level `authorize()` policy datastore — still a stated seam (`Authorizer` interface), real implementation is a later story per `handoff-03-auth.md` v3's F8 resolution.
38	- No client-credential provisioning UI/API for new callers — out of scope for this take-home entirely, per `01-product-industry-research-design`'s track boundary.
39	
40	## Open question — resolved
41	
42	**Original question:** `backlog.md` had no story for `/auth/token`'s actual grant logic (validate client_id/client_secret, mint a signed JWT) — LT-32 only built the route as a 501 placeholder, and nothing downstream picked up the real logic. Tobias's objection sharpened this from "a gap this refinement can flag and work around" to "a missing story, not a pending one" — unlike LT-36/37's wait on LT-38, or LT-32's wait on LT-34, this had no ticket and no scheduled end, and treating an out-of-band token as this story's own review method risked quietly weakening "done = deployed and reviewable at a public URL" into "done = reviewable by an engineer with cluster access," without anyone deciding that on purpose.
43	
44	**Resolution (team-lead):** confirmed as a real gap and a new story, not scope creep and not "out of scope forever." **LT-51** (Epic LT-3, security-graded, linked LT-40 → blocks → LT-51) — OAuth2 client-credentials grant at `/auth/token`, per Marcus's `handoff-03-auth.md` v4 addendum (now landed): HTTP Basic client auth, form-encoded `client_credentials` grant per RFC 6749 §4.4, §5.1/§5.2 token/error responses with an identical `invalid_client` for unknown-client vs. wrong-secret (no oracle distinguishing the two), client store in the issuer's own datastore with Argon2id-hashed secrets, a `jti` claim recommended for audit correlation only (not a revocation mechanism — TTL-only revocation stands per `api-auth-design.md`), and the public key distributed via a JWKS endpoint served by the issuer's in-cluster `ClusterIP` `Service` — no public `Ingress` route for it, since only the verifying middleware inside the cluster needs it. 03-owned code, 02-supplied spec. Refined right after this story.
45	
46	**Corrected ruling on the review token, replacing the earlier "out-of-band" framing:** there is no path where the private key signs anything outside the issuer process, not even for review. LT-40's live-URL review therefore happens *together with* LT-51's, using a real token obtained from a real `/auth/token` call, authenticated with a dedicated, narrowly-scoped QA client credential seeded specifically for this purpose (LT-51's own criteria, below) — not a workaround token and not a separate acceptance event.
47	
48	## PO review script
49	
50	1. Confirm the algorithm-allowlist and `kid`-rotation unit tests exist and pass — read the test names and assertions directly, not just "tests pass."
51	2. Confirm `AuthContext` population uses the already-exported `WithAuthContext`/`AuthContext` types from `internal/api`, not a parallel/duplicate type — grep for a second definition of anything auth-context-shaped.
52	3. Confirm the issuer-side rate limiting is a distinct code path from the resource-API rate limiter already in `internal/api/ratelimit.go` — this is genuinely new work, shouldn't reuse or be confused with the existing one.
53	4. Confirm the context-propagation deadline is a concrete, named number in code (e.g. a constant), not an inline magic value scattered across call sites.
54	5. **Run jointly with LT-51's review, once both are deployed:** obtain a real token from `/auth/token` using the seeded QA client credential (LT-51's criteria), then hit `/profiles/search` and `/profiles/{id}` at the live URL, confirming a masked response, a 401 on a missing/expired/wrong-audience token, a 403 on a wrong-scope token, and — if enough calls are made — an actual 429 once the touch cap is crossed. This is the live-URL review LT-34/LT-39/LT-40 have all been waiting on; LT-40 and LT-51 go Done together in this one pass, not separately.
55	
56	## Research team review
57	
58	**Desmond Okafor — claims check:** Re-verified directly against PR #26's actual diff (not the doc's own word) that `Deps`/`RateLimiter`/`TouchCounter` are handler-level, not middleware — confirmed, narrowing was accurate. Two minor status fixes: "that's LT-39, already merged" was wrong (PR #26 is still open) — fixed; the doc header cited only v3 when the body already treats v4 as landed — fixed to cite v4. All LT-51/v4-addendum claims (RFC 6749 §4.4, identical `invalid_client`, Argon2id hashing, `jti` non-revocation, JWKS in-cluster-only, the no-out-of-band-signing ruling) verified word-for-word against `handoff-03-auth.md` v4. One footnote flagged to Renata separately, not a defect here: `decisions/context-propagation.md` cites its own ruling as living in "S3's middleware design," using a numbering scheme that predates current `backlog.md` IDs (current S3 is LT-36, not this story) — LT-40.md doesn't repeat that stale label, but a future reader chasing "S3" there would land on the wrong story.
59	
60	**Tobias Lindqvist — objection:** The missing token-issuance logic wasn't a "pending, scheduled" gap like LT-36/37 waiting on LT-38 — it had no ticket at all, and treating an out-of-band token as this story's own review method would have quietly weakened "done = reviewable at a public URL" into "done = reviewable by an engineer with cluster access," without anyone deciding that on purpose. Resolved: a new story (LT-51) was created rather than worked around, sequenced right after this one, with LT-40's live-URL review folded into LT-51's joint review using a real issued token — not a separate, lesser acceptance event.
61	
62	**Naomi Voss — ruling:** All fixes applied (status accuracy, header citation, the stray-numbering flag routed to Renata). This refinement is accepted and ready to hand off. Between this story and LT-51, the review chain caught something more consequential than a citation error: an entire missing story that a lesser process would have quietly worked around with a review shortcut, permanently softening the project's own "done" bar without anyone choosing that on purpose.
63

The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note> <result>Findings from re-reviewing PR #26 at commit f93b19d (`gh pr diff 26 --repo wtvamp/loginid-takehome`, full current diff). 1. Resolved, with a real test. `internal/api/handlers.go` — `NewGetProfileHandler` only calls `d.TouchCounter.Reserve` inside `if ac.Scope == ScopeReadAny`; for `ScopeReadOwn`, `reserved` stays its zero value (`false`) and every downstream `if reserved { Settle }` guard correctly skips. `TestGetProfileHandler_ReadOwn_DoesNotHitTouchCap` (handlers_test.go) locks this in: it wires a `TouchCounter` that denies everything and asserts 200 for `read:own`. That's what I asked for — not just a fixed code path but a test that fails if it regresses. 2. Consistent now. Every DAO call error (`d.Repo.Profiles().Search`/`.Get`) goes through `writeDAOError`; every non-DAO collaborator error (`RateLimiter.Allow`, `TouchCounter.Reserve`/`Settle`, `Authorizer.Authorize`) goes through `writeInternalError`. The comment on `writeInternalError` in `errors.go` now names exactly why it's kept separate rather than reusing `writeDAOError` — my prior naming-risk objection is addressed in the doc comment itself, not just in behavior. 3. Resolved. `handlers.go`'s comment above the `reasonCode` block in `NewGetProfileHandler` now states explicitly: it's validated and logged for the audit trail, has no other consumer this story, and threading it into `Authorizer` is a follow-up story's concern. That's the traceability I was missing. 4. One new issue, narrow but real, in the Reserve/Settle delta itself. I traced every exit point in both handlers — `NewSearchHandler` and `NewGetProfileHandler` — and every successful `Reserve` does get exactly one matching `Settle` call on every code path (Authorize error, Authorize denial, DAO error, and success), including the `read:any`-only conditional in the get handler. No leaked or double-released reservations there; the concurrency tests (`TestInProcessTouchCounter_ConcurrentReservesNeverExceedCap` with `-race`, `TestInProcessTouchCounter_ReserveBooksBudgetBeforeSettle`) exercise the actual race Nolan flagged, not a sequential stand-in. The issue is what context those cleanup `Settle` calls run under. Every `Settle` call on an error-exit path (e.g. `handlers.go:761`, `766`, `782`, `895`, `902`, `912`) uses `ctx := r.Context()` — the same context that may already be canceled or past its deadline, since these are precisely the failure paths where an upstream call (DAO, Authorize, RateLimiter) just errored, plausibly because that context expired. `decisions/context-propagation.md` already established the principle that a release/durability write shouldn't inherit a context that's about to make it fail — that ruling scoped it to DAO credential writes, but `Settle`-as-release is functionally the same shape: a cleanup operation that must succeed even when the triggering request context is dying. Today this is harmless because `InProcessTouchCounter.Reserve`/`Settle` both ignore `ctx` entirely (first parameter is `_ context.Context`). But the moment this interface is backed by something real (Redis, per the type's own doc comment), a canceled context handed to a cleanup `Settle` call could make the release itself fail, permanently leaking reserved budget until the window's sweep — silently changing the whole cap's behavior under load. Same pattern as the writeDAOError/writeInternalError naming risk I raised last round: safe today, latent for the next person who fills in the interface. Recommend detaching the context (`context.WithoutCancel`) specifically at these cleanup `Settle` call sites, or a comment on `TouchCounter.Settle`'s doc stating callers must not assume its `ctx` argument is honored for cancellation — whichever Renata prefers, but it shouldn't be silent. Relevant files: `internal/api/handlers.go`, `internal/api/errors.go`, `internal/api/touchcounter.go`, `internal/api/touchcounter_test.go`, `internal/api/handlers_test.go`, `03-engineering-delivery/decisions/context-propagation.md`.</result> <usage><subagent_tokens>80996</subagent_tokens><tool_uses>9</tool_uses><duration_ms>82404</duration_ms></usage> </task-notification>

1	package api
2	
3	import (
4		"context"
5		"encoding/json"
6		"net/http"
7		"strings"
8		"time"
9	
10		"loginid-takehome/internal/dao"
11	)
12	
13	// Deps bundles a handler's collaborators — the DAO, the object-level
14	// policy hook, the two independent limiters, and the audit sink — so
15	// NewSearchHandler/NewGetProfileHandler take one argument instead of
16	// five, and a test fake for any one of them doesn't require constructing
17	// the other four by hand each time.
18	type Deps struct {
19		Repo         dao.Repository
20		Authz        Authorizer
21		RateLimiter  RateLimiter
22		TouchCounter TouchCounter
23		AuditLog     AuditLogger
24	}
25	
26	const (
27		defaultPageSize = 20
28		maxPageSize     = 50
29		readRateLimit   = 60 // req/min, read:own and read:any
30		searchRateLimit = 10 // req/min, profile:search
31	)
32	
33	type searchRequest struct {
34		Name     *string `json:"name,omitempty"`
35		Phone    *string `json:"phone,omitempty"`
36		Region   *string `json:"region,omitempty"`
37		Country  *string `json:"country,omitempty"`
38		Cursor   string  `json:"cursor,omitempty"`
39		PageSize int     `json:"page_size,omitempty"`
40	}
41	
42	type searchResponse struct {
43		Results    []SearchResultProfile `json:"results"`
44		Total      int                   `json:"total"`
45		NextCursor string                `json:"next_cursor,omitempty"`
46	}
47	
48	// auditCtx is the small bundle handler code passes to Deps.audit — a
49	// context.Context plus the sub/scope an audit event needs, since those
50	// two are read from AuthContext once per request rather than threaded as
51	// separate parameters to every call. sub/scope are zero-valued for the
52	// one case that precedes reading AuthContext at all (a missing/invalid
53	// token).
54	type auditCtx struct {
55		ctx   context.Context
56		sub   string
57		scope Scope
58	}
59	
60	func (d Deps) audit(ac auditCtx, kind AuditEventKind, recordIDs []string, reasonCode ReasonCode) {
61		d.AuditLog.Log(ac.ctx, AuditEvent{
62			Sub:        ac.sub,
63			Scope:      ac.scope,
64			Kind:       kind,
65			RecordIDs:  recordIDs,
66			ReasonCode: reasonCode,
67			Timestamp:  time.Now().UTC(),
68		})
69	}
70	
71	// NewSearchHandler implements profile:search (handoff-03-auth.md v3).
72	// Order of checks, deliberately, and matching NewGetProfileHandler's
73	// order below (shape validation before either limiter, both limiters
74	// before authorize()) rather than the two handlers disagreeing on it for
75	// no stated reason (Oren Castellan, PR #26 review): auth context present
76	// -> scope is ScopeSearch -> request shape (unknown fields, empty body,
77	// page_size cap) -> per-minute rate limit -> cumulative touch cap ->
78	// authorize() -> the DAO call -> settle touches -> masked response. Scope
79	// mismatch and an authorize() denial return an identical client-visible
80	// body (F7); the audit log below is the only place that distinction is
81	// allowed to exist (Ingrid Solano, PR #26 review — this story's
82	// acceptance criteria require the distinction to be logged, not merely
83	// kept out of the response).
84	func NewSearchHandler(d Deps) http.HandlerFunc {
85		return func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
86			ctx := r.Context()
87			ac, ok := AuthContextFromContext(ctx)
88			if !ok {
89				d.audit(auditCtx{ctx: ctx}, AuditAuthnFailure, nil, "")
90				writeUnauthorized(w)
91				return
92			}
93			actx := auditCtx{ctx: ctx, sub: ac.Sub, scope: ac.Scope}
94			if ac.Scope != ScopeSearch {
95				d.audit(actx, AuditScopeFailure, nil, "")
96				writeForbidden(w)
97				return
98			}
99	
100			var req searchRequest
101			if r.Body != nil {
102				dec := json.NewDecoder(r.Body)
103				// Reject any field this shape doesn't name — in particular
104				// an "offset" key, which would otherwise be silently
105				// ignored rather than rejected. The API-facing contract is
106				// cursor-only pagination (handoff-03-auth.md v3's F-pag);
107				// a caller-supplied offset-shaped parameter is exactly the
108				// bypass that contract exists to close, so it must fail
109				// loudly, not be dropped quietly (Ingrid Solano, PR #26
110				// review).
111				dec.DisallowUnknownFields()
112				if err := dec.Decode(&req); err != nil {
113					writeBadRequest(w, "malformed request body or unrecognized field")
114					return
115				}
116			}
117	
118			// A search request with no filter field at all is rejected here
119			// with a 400 before it ever reaches the DAO — the DAO's own
120			// all-nil ProfileQuery is legal and returns every profile, which
121			// this API must not expose directly (handoff-03-auth.md v3, F10).
122			if req.Name == nil && req.Phone == nil && req.Region == nil && req.Country == nil {
123				writeBadRequest(w, "at least one search filter is required")
124				return
125			}
126	
127			offset, err := decodeCursor(req.Cursor)
128			if err != nil {
129				writeBadRequest(w, "malformed cursor")
130				return
131			}
132	
133			pageSize := req.PageSize
134			if pageSize <= 0 {
135				pageSize = defaultPageSize
136			}
137			if pageSize > maxPageSize {
138				// A bare/unconstrained request for more than this API's
139				// result cap is rejected outright, not truncated
140				// (handoff-03-auth.md v3) — checked here, before the query
141				// is built, mirroring the same rule for authorize() below.
142				writeBadRequest(w, "page_size exceeds the maximum of 50")
143				return
144			}
145	
146			allow, err := d.RateLimiter.Allow(ctx, ac.Sub, ScopeSearch, searchRateLimit)
147			if err != nil {
148				writeInternalError(w, err)
149				return
150			}
151			if !allow {
152				d.audit(actx, AuditRateLimited, nil, "")
153				writeRateLimited(w)
154				return
155			}
156	
157			// Reserve, not just check: this books pageSize's worth of budget
158			// against the window immediately, atomically with the capacity
159			// check, so a second concurrent request from the same sub sees
160			// this reservation and is correctly denied before either
161			// request's DAO call returns — closing a check-then-act race a
162			// separate Allowed()-then-RecordTouches() pair had, which could
163			// let N concurrent requests each add a full page's worth of
164			// touches before any of them recorded (Nolan Reyes, PR #26
165			// review). Every exit past this point must call Settle exactly
166			// once to release it.
167			reserved, err := d.TouchCounter.Reserve(ctx, ac.Sub, pageSize)
168			if err != nil {
169				writeInternalError(w, err)
170				return
171			}
172			if !reserved {
173				d.audit(actx, AuditCapExceeded, nil, "")
174				writeTouchCapExceeded(w)
175				return
176			}
177	
178			// The object-level policy hook, per v3: called after scope
179			// validation (above) and request-shape validation (above), before
180			// the DAO call. ScopeSearch has no single target id.
181			allowed, err := d.Authz.Authorize(ctx, ac.Sub, ac.Scope, "")
182			if err != nil {
183				releaseReservation(ctx, d.TouchCounter, ac.Sub, pageSize)
184				writeInternalError(w, err)
185				return
186			}
187			if !allowed {
188				releaseReservation(ctx, d.TouchCounter, ac.Sub, pageSize)
189				d.audit(actx, AuditPolicyDenial, nil, "")
190				writeForbidden(w)
191				return
192			}
193	
194			q := dao.ProfileQuery{
195				Name:    req.Name,
196				Phone:   req.Phone,
197				Region:  req.Region,
198				Country: req.Country,
199				Limit:   pageSize,
200				Offset:  offset,
201			}
202			results, total, err := d.Repo.Profiles().Search(ctx, q)
203			if err != nil {
204				releaseReservation(ctx, d.TouchCounter, ac.Sub, pageSize)
205				d.audit(actx, AuditRequestFailed, nil, "")
206				writeDAOError(w, err)
207				return
208			}
209	
210			ids := make([]string, len(results))
211			masked := make([]SearchResultProfile, len(results))
212			for i, p := range results {
213				ids[i] = p.ID
214				masked[i] = toSearchResult(p)
215			}
216			if err := d.TouchCounter.Settle(ctx, ac.Sub, ids, pageSize); err != nil {
217				writeInternalError(w, err)
218				return
219			}
220	
221			d.audit(actx, AuditSuccess, ids, "")
222			resp := searchResponse{Results: masked, Total: total}
223			if offset+len(results) < total {
224				resp.NextCursor = encodeCursor(offset + len(results))
225			}
226			writeJSON(w, http.StatusOK, resp)
227		}
228	}
229	
230	// NewGetProfileHandler implements profile:read:own and profile:read:any
231	// against the same handler — the two scopes differ in what Authorize()
232	// checks (referral membership vs. a logged reason_code) and in whether
233	// the cumulative touch cap applies at all. id is read from the request
234	// path via r.PathValue("id") (Go 1.22+ ServeMux pattern, e.g.
235	// "GET /profiles/{id}"); this handler is not yet registered on any real
236	// route (refinement/LT-39.md's Acceptance outcome — deferred until S7's
237	// auth middleware exists to protect it), so tests call it directly with
238	// http.Request.SetPathValue.
239	func NewGetProfileHandler(d Deps) http.HandlerFunc {
240		return func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
241			ctx := r.Context()
242			ac, ok := AuthContextFromContext(ctx)
243			if !ok {
244				d.audit(auditCtx{ctx: ctx}, AuditAuthnFailure, nil, "")
245				writeUnauthorized(w)
246				return
247			}
248			actx := auditCtx{ctx: ctx, sub: ac.Sub, scope: ac.Scope}
249			if ac.Scope != ScopeReadOwn && ac.Scope != ScopeReadAny {
250				d.audit(actx, AuditScopeFailure, nil, "")
251				writeForbidden(w)
252				return
253			}
254	
255			id := r.PathValue("id")
256			if strings.TrimSpace(id) == "" {
257				writeBadRequest(w, "id is required")
258				return
259			}
260	
261			// reasonCode is validated here and logged with every audit event
262			// on this request (handoff-03-auth.md v3: "reason_code, when the
263			// scope is profile:read:any" is a required audit-log field).
264			// There is deliberately no other consumer of the value — no
265			// policy decision in this story reads it beyond validating its
266			// membership in the closed enum; threading it into Authorizer's
267			// signature is a follow-up story's job if a real policy
268			// implementation ever needs it for more than logging (Oren
269			// Castellan, PR #26 review).
270			var reasonCode ReasonCode
271			if ac.Scope == ScopeReadAny {
272				reasonCode = ReasonCode(r.URL.Query().Get("reason_code"))
273				if !reasonCode.Valid() {
274					writeBadRequest(w, "reason_code is required for profile:read:any and must be one of the closed enum values")
275					return
276				}
277			}
278	
279			allow, err := d.RateLimiter.Allow(ctx, ac.Sub, ac.Scope, readRateLimit)
280			if err != nil {
281				writeInternalError(w, err)
282				return
283			}
284			if !allow {
285				d.audit(actx, AuditRateLimited, nil, reasonCode)
286				writeRateLimited(w)
287				return
288			}
289	
290			// The cumulative cap applies to profile:search and
291			// profile:read:any (handoff-03-auth.md v3, verbatim) — not
292			// profile:read:own, which is bounded by the caller_referral
293			// relationship rather than an open id space, so it isn't the
294			// enumeration surface this control exists for. A valid, logged
295			// reason_code on read:any satisfies Authorize() below but does
296			// not exempt the caller from this cap — the exact regression v3
297			// fixed (an earlier wording made this counter a no-op for
298			// read:any, since that scope requires a reason_code on every call
299			// by design).
300			var reserved bool
301			if ac.Scope == ScopeReadAny {
302				reserved, err = d.TouchCounter.Reserve(ctx, ac.Sub, 1)
303				if err != nil {
304					writeInternalError(w, err)
305					return
306				}
307				if !reserved {
308					d.audit(actx, AuditCapExceeded, nil, reasonCode)
309					writeTouchCapExceeded(w)
310					return
311				}
312			}
313	
314			allowed, err := d.Authz.Authorize(ctx, ac.Sub, ac.Scope, id)
315			if err != nil {
316				if reserved {
317					_ = d.TouchCounter.Settle(ctx, ac.Sub, nil, 1)
318				}
319				writeInternalError(w, err)
320				return
321			}
322			if !allowed {
323				if reserved {
324					_ = d.TouchCounter.Settle(ctx, ac.Sub, nil, 1)
325				}
326				d.audit(actx, AuditPolicyDenial, []string{id}, reasonCode)
327				writeForbidden(w)
328				return
329			}
330	
331			p, err := d.Repo.Profiles().Get(ctx, id)
332			if err != nil {
333				if reserved {
334					_ = d.TouchCounter.Settle(ctx, ac.Sub, nil, 1)
335				}
336				d.audit(actx, AuditRequestFailed, []string{id}, reasonCode)
337				writeDAOError(w, err)
338				return
339			}
340	
341			if reserved {
342				if err := d.TouchCounter.Settle(ctx, ac.Sub, []string{p.ID}, 1); err != nil {
343					writeInternalError(w, err)
344					return
345				}
346			}
347	
348			d.audit(actx, AuditSuccess, []string{p.ID}, reasonCode)
349			writeJSON(w, http.StatusOK, toProfileResponse(p))
350		}
351	}
352	
353	func writeJSON(w http.ResponseWriter, status int, body any) {
354		w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
355		w.WriteHeader(status)
356		_ = json.NewEncoder(w).Encode(body)
357	}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-worktrees/LT-39/internal/api/touchcounter.go",
  "old_string": "func (c *InProcessTouchCounter) Settle(_ context.Context, sub string, recordIDs []string, reserved int) error {",
  "new_string": "// touchReservation guarantees a booked TouchCounter reservation is\n// released exactly once, by a deferred cleanup unless settle() already\n// ran — closing the \"a manual Settle call at every return path\" fragility\n// (Nolan Reyes, PR #26 review): the next handler that reserves and\n// forgets one branch's Settle call silently burns that caller's budget\n// for the rest of the window, and nothing catches it, including a panic\n// unwinding past a manual call. A defer can't be forgotten the same way.\ntype touchReservation struct {\n\ttc       TouchCounter\n\tsub      string\n\treserved int\n\tdone     bool\n}\n\n// reserveTouches books maxNewTouches against sub's window if the cap\n// allows it. ok is false if the cap is already met — the caller must not\n// use the returned *touchReservation in that case (it is nil).\nfunc reserveTouches(ctx context.Context, tc TouchCounter, sub string, maxNewTouches int) (tr *touchReservation, ok bool, err error) {\n\tok, err = tc.Reserve(ctx, sub, maxNewTouches)\n\tif err != nil || !ok {\n\t\treturn nil, ok, err\n\t}\n\treturn &touchReservation{tc: tc, sub: sub, reserved: maxNewTouches}, true, nil\n}\n\n// release is a no-op if settle already ran; call it via `defer\n// tr.release(ctx)` immediately after a successful reserveTouches, so\n// every return path — including one added later without updating this\n// function, and a panic unwind — releases an unsettled reservation.\n// Detaches ctx from the triggering request's own cancellation\n// (context.WithoutCancel): this is a release-shaped operation exactly\n// like the DAO credential-write detach points decisions/\n// context-propagation.md already rules on, and the most common reason\n// this fires at all is that ctx just expired or was canceled upstream —\n// handing that same dying context to the cleanup would be handing the\n// release the same failure the request itself just hit (Oren Castellan,\n// PR #26 review).\nfunc (tr *touchReservation) release(ctx context.Context) {\n\tif tr == nil || tr.done {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\ttr.done = true\n\t_ = tr.tc.Settle(context.WithoutCancel(ctx), tr.sub, nil, tr.reserved)\n}\n\n// settle records the actual distinct record ids touched and marks the\n// reservation finalized, so the deferred release becomes a no-op.\nfunc (tr *touchReservation) settle(ctx context.Context, recordIDs []string) error {\n\tif tr == nil || tr.done {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\ttr.done = true\n\treturn tr.tc.Settle(ctx, tr.sub, recordIDs, tr.reserved)\n}\n\nfunc (c *InProcessTouchCounter) Settle(_ context.Context, sub string, recordIDs []string, reserved int) error {"
}

  "new_string": "\t\t// Reserve, not just check: this books pageSize's worth of budget\n\t\t// against the window immediately, atomically with the capacity\n\t\t// check, so a second concurrent request from the same sub sees\n\t\t// this reservation and is correctly denied before either\n\t\t// request's DAO call returns — closing a check-then-act race a\n\t\t// separate Allowed()-then-RecordTouches() pair had, which could\n\t\t// let N concurrent requests each add a full page's worth of\n\t\t// touches before any of them recorded (Nolan Reyes, PR #26\n\t\t// review). The deferred release below guarantees this is settled\n\t\t// exactly once on every exit path — including one added later\n\t\t// without an explicit Settle call, and a panic unwind — rather\n\t\t// than relying on a manual Settle call at each return (Nolan\n\t\t// Reyes, second-round review).\n\t\ttr, reserved, err := reserveTouches(ctx, d.TouchCounter, ac.Sub, pageSize)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\twriteInternalError(w, err)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tif !reserved {\n\t\t\td.audit(actx, AuditCapExceeded, nil, \"\")\n\t\t\twriteTouchCapExceeded(w)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tdefer tr.release(ctx)\n\n\t\t// The object-level policy hook, per v3: called after scope\n\t\t// validation (above) and request-shape validation (above), before\n\t\t// the DAO call. ScopeSearch has no single target id.\n\t\tallowed, err := d.Authz.Authorize(ctx, ac.Sub, ac.Scope, \"\")\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\twriteInternalError(w, err)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tif !allowed {\n\t\t\td.audit(actx, AuditPolicyDenial, nil, \"\")\n\t\t\twriteForbidden(w)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tq := dao.ProfileQuery{\n\t\t\tName:    req.Name,\n\t\t\tPhone:   req.Phone,\n\t\t\tRegion:  req.Region,\n\t\t\tCountry: req.Country,\n\t\t\tLimit:   pageSize,\n\t\t\tOffset:  offset,\n\t\t}\n\t\tresults, total, err := d.Repo.Profiles().Search(ctx, q)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\td.audit(actx, AuditRequestFailed, nil, \"\")\n\t\t\twriteDAOError(w, err)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tids := make([]string, len(results))\n\t\tmasked := make([]SearchResultProfile, len(results))\n\t\tfor i, p := range results {\n\t\t\tids[i] = p.ID\n\t\t\tmasked[i] = toSearchResult(p)\n\t\t}\n\t\tif err := tr.settle(ctx, ids); err != nil {\n\t\t\twriteInternalError(w, err)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}"

func TestGetProfileHandler_NotFound(t *testing.T) {
	d := allowAllDeps()
	repo := d.Repo.(*fakeRepository)
	repo.profiles.getFn = func(ctx context.Context, id string) (*model.UserProfile, error) {
		return nil, dao.ErrNotFound
	}
	h := NewGetProfileHandler(d)
	req := newGetRequest("11111111-1111-1111-1111-111111111111")
	req = req.WithContext(withScope("client1", ScopeReadOwn))
	w := httptest.NewRecorder()
	h(w, req)
	if w.Code != http.StatusNotFound {
		t.Errorf("status = %d, want 404", w.Code)
	}
}

{
  "command": "cd /Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-worktrees/LT-39 && git add internal/api && git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nfix: defer-based touch-reservation release, detached cleanup context (round 2)\n\nOren Castellan and Nolan Reyes, re-reviewing the Reserve/Settle redesign\nfrom the prior fix commit, both confirmed the concurrency race is\ngenuinely closed (Nolan: traced the lock structure directly, confirmed\nReserve's capacity-check-and-book happens under one critical section\nwith no visibility gap; TestInProcessTouchCounter_ConcurrentReservesNeverExceedCap\nfires 20 real goroutines under -race and proves it). Two follow-on\nfindings, both fixed here:\n\nOren: every cleanup Settle call on an error-exit path reused ctx — the\nsame context whose expiry or cancellation is often exactly why the\npreceding call (Authorize, the DAO call) just failed. Reserve/Settle's\nown doc comment already says a real backend (Redis) could then fail the\nrelease itself under a dying context, silently leaking reserved budget\nuntil the window's own sweep. Same principle decisions/\ncontext-propagation.md already applies to DAO credential writes,\none layer up.\n\nNolan: every Settle call was manually placed at each handler return\npath rather than derived from one release point — safe today because\nevery existing path was traced by hand, but fragile by construction: the\nnext early return added to either handler without an accompanying\nSettle call silently burns that caller's cumulative-cap budget for the\nrest of the 24h window, and a panic between Reserve and any Settle call\ndoes the same with no recovery.\n\nFixed both together with one change: touchcounter.go gains\ntouchReservation, a small guard returned by reserveTouches() alongside\nReserve's bool result. Handler code now does\n`defer tr.release(ctx)` immediately after a successful reserve, and\n`tr.settle(ctx, ids)` on the success path — settle marks the reservation\nfinalized so the deferred release becomes a no-op; every other exit\n(error, denial, or an outright panic) is covered by the defer with no\nper-branch Settle call required. release() itself calls Settle through\ncontext.WithoutCancel(ctx), addressing Oren's finding directly. Both\nhandlers' manual Settle-at-every-branch code is gone, replaced by one\nReserve+defer pair each.\n\nTestSearchHandler_PanicStillReleasesTouchReservation proves the guarantee\nNolan's finding was about: it panics from inside Authorize, recovers only\nat the test boundary (not inside the handler — nothing in the handler\nneeds a recover for this to work), and asserts the reservation was still\nreleased. TestSearchHandler_DeniedRequestReleasesTouchReservation\n(previous commit) continues to pass unchanged against the new\nimplementation.\n\nVerified: go build/vet clean, go test ./internal/api/... -race green (34\ntests), gofmt clean, full repo suite unaffected.\n\nStory: LT-39\nWork-By: Renata Cole (lead), reviewed by Oren Castellan and Nolan Reyes\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>\nClaude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_011Cuba4JUvrsQCrTdUj4yzk\nEOF\n)\""

3:Status: **v4** — adds the token-issuance grant logic (below), a gap Naomi Voss found during LT-40 refinement: v3 specified verification and scopes in full but never specified `/auth/token`'s actual grant handling, and LT-32 shipped that endpoint as a `501` placeholder with no issuance story behind it. Everything from v3 is unchanged. Receiver: Renata Cole, `../03-engineering-delivery/`. Written so 03 should be able to scaffold and build the middleware without re-deriving the reasoning in `api-auth-design.md`, `connector-security.md`, or the S3/S4/S8 decision records. Full reasoning, if wanted: `api-auth-design.md`, `decisions/search-authz-scoping.md`, `decisions/connector-token-lifecycle-redblue.md`.
5:## Token issuance (`POST /auth/token`) — v4 addendum
19:**JWT claims issued:** `iss` = `https://auth.loginid-takehome.internal`; `aud` = `loginid-api-service` or `idp-connector-service` depending on which audience the authenticated client is registered for (a client is provisioned for one audience, not both); `sub` = `client_id`; `scope` = the granted-or-requested set, space-delimited; `exp`/`iat` per the 5–15 min TTL already specified; header `kid` identifying the signing key version. **`jti` (recommended, not required):** a random per-token identifier, included solely as an audit-log correlation aid (so a token's issuance and its later use can be joined in logs) — it is never checked against a denylist at verification time, since this design already ruled out stateful revocation in favor of short TTLs; including it costs nothing statefully and is worth the correlation value.
21:**Public-key distribution for the verifier (enforcement site, your call was open, ruled here): JWKS served by the issuer Deployment, reachable only in-cluster via its own `ClusterIP` Service — no Ingress route, never publicly reachable.** Not a mounted public-key file: JWKS survives key rotation without redeploying the verifying Deployment's config, since the verifier fetches by `kid` and only needs to know the issuer Service's in-cluster DNS name (`<issuer-service>.<namespace>.svc.cluster.local`), not track individual key files. In-cluster-only because this token scheme's only consumers are `api-service` and `idp-connector`, both already inside the cluster network; there's no reason to add a new publicly-reachable route on a shared production cluster (`threat-model.md` Assumption 7) for a key set that only internal services ever need. Verifier caches the fetched key set in memory with a short TTL (matching token TTL is reasonable) to avoid a JWKS fetch on every single verification.
28:- **Token format:** JWT, signed, asymmetric (RS256 or ES256 — pick one and use it consistently; RS256 is the safer default if your Go JWT library's ES256 support is less mature).
33:- **Verifier must pin the expected algorithm** (whichever of RS256/ES256 you choose) and reject a token whose header claims any other algorithm, including `alg: none` and a downgrade to a symmetric algorithm using the public key as an HMAC secret (RFC 8725 §3.1). Check your JWT library's default behavior — several popular libraries have shipped this vulnerability by not doing this by default.
40:- **Key retrieval:** middleware verifies with the **public** key only (JWKS or a fetched public key — see `handoff-04-secrets.md` row #3). The private signing key never touches the verifying `api-service` Deployment; it lives with the token-issuing function, which is a mode of the same `api-service` image but runs as its **own Deployment** (`APP_MODE=issuer`, its own ServiceAccount), not the same Deployment doing both (corrected in `handoff-04-secrets.md` Assumption 2 and row #2, F13 — RBAC on a Secret's `get` verb doesn't stop a volume already mounted into a shared Deployment's containers, so the separation has to be a second Deployment, not RBAC alone). This is a second `APP_MODE` value for you to wire alongside your existing config surface.
41:- **Key rotation:** expect a `kid` claim; publish old and new public keys together during rotation overlap. Token TTL (5–15 min) bounds how long that overlap needs to last.

Now building the JWT verification core: JWKS parsing/caching, claims, and the middleware.
Write /Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-worktrees/LT-40/internal/api/jwks.go
input
{
  "file_path": "/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-worktrees/LT-40/internal/api/jwks.go",
  "content": "// jwks.go implements a minimal RFC 7517 JSON Web Key Set client: fetch,\n// parse RSA public keys, cache with a short TTL. handoff-03-auth.md v4:\n// \"Public-key distribution for the verifier ... JWKS served by the issuer\n// Deployment, reachable only in-cluster via its own ClusterIP Service ...\n// Verifier caches the fetched key set in memory with a short TTL (matching\n// token TTL is reasonable) to avoid a JWKS fetch on every single\n// verification.\"\npackage api\n\nimport (\n\t\"crypto/rsa\"\n\t\"encoding/base64\"\n\t\"encoding/json\"\n\t\"fmt\"\n\t\"math/big\"\n\t\"net/http\"\n\t\"sync\"\n\t\"time\"\n)\n\n// jwk is one entry in a JWKS response — only the fields this verifier\n// needs to reconstruct an RSA public key (RFC 7517 §4, RFC 7518 §6.3.1).\n// Fields this verifier doesn't use (x5c, x5t, ...) are simply not\n// unmarshaled; unknown fields in the source JSON are not an error.\ntype jwk struct {\n\tKty string `json:\"kty\"`\n\tKid string `json:\"kid\"`\n\tAlg string `json:\"alg\"`\n\tUse string `json:\"use\"`\n\tN   string `json:\"n\"` // base64url, unpadded — the RSA modulus\n\tE   string `json:\"e\"` // base64url, unpadded — the RSA public exponent\n}\n\ntype jwksResponse struct {\n\tKeys []jwk `json:\"keys\"`\n}\n\n// parseJWKS decodes a JWKS JSON document into a map of kid -> public key.\n// Any key with kty != \"RSA\" is skipped (not an error) — a future non-RSA\n// key in the set (e.g. during an algorithm migration) shouldn't break\n// verification of tokens signed with the RSA keys that ARE present.\nfunc parseJWKS(data []byte) (map[string]*rsa.PublicKey, error) {\n\tvar resp jwksResponse\n\tif err := json.Unmarshal(data, &resp); err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"api: parsing JWKS: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\tkeys := make(map[string]*rsa.PublicKey, len(resp.Keys))\n\tfor _, k := range resp.Keys {\n\t\tif k.Kty != \"RSA\" {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tif k.Kid == \"\" {\n\t\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"api: JWKS RSA key missing kid\")\n\t\t}\n\t\tpub, err := rsaPublicKeyFromJWK(k)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"api: JWKS key %q: %w\", k.Kid, err)\n\t\t}\n\t\tkeys[k.Kid] = pub\n\t}\n\treturn keys, nil\n}\n\nfunc rsaPublicKeyFromJWK(k jwk) (*rsa.PublicKey, error) {\n\tnBytes, err := base64.RawURLEncoding.DecodeString(k.N)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"decoding n: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\teBytes, err := base64.RawURLEncoding.DecodeString(k.E)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"decoding e: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\tn := new(big.Int).SetBytes(nBytes)\n\te := new(big.Int).SetBytes(eBytes)\n\tif !e.IsInt64() {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"exponent out of range\")\n\t}\n\treturn &rsa.PublicKey{N: n, E: int(e.Int64())}, nil\n}\n\n// KeySource resolves a kid to the RSA public key that should verify a\n// token carrying it. JWKSCache is the real implementation; tests use a\n// map-backed fake.\ntype KeySource interface {\n\tKeyForKid(kid string) (*rsa.PublicKey, error)\n}\n\n// JWKSCache fetches a JWKS document from url and caches the parsed key\n// set for ttl, refetching only after it expires — \"to avoid a JWKS fetch\n// on every single verification\" (handoff-03-auth.md v4). Supports at\n// least two concurrently valid kids during a rotation overlap window by\n// construction: it caches the WHOLE key set the issuer publishes, not\n// just the most recent key, so an old and a new key both verify\n// successfully for as long as the issuer's own JWKS response lists both.\ntype JWKSCache struct {\n\turl        string\n\tttl        time.Duration\n\thttpClient *http.Client\n\n\tmu        sync.Mutex\n\tkeys      map[string]*rsa.PublicKey\n\tfetchedAt time.Time\n}\n\n// NewJWKSCache constructs a cache fetching from url, refetching at most\n// once per ttl. A nil httpClient uses http.DefaultClient.\nfunc NewJWKSCache(url string, ttl time.Duration, httpClient *http.Client) *JWKSCache {\n\tif httpClient == nil {\n\t\thttpClient = http.DefaultClient\n\t}\n\treturn &JWKSCache{url: url, ttl: ttl, httpClient: httpClient}\n}\n\nfunc (c *JWKSCache) KeyForKid(kid string) (*rsa.PublicKey, error) {\n\tc.mu.Lock()\n\tdefer c.mu.Unlock()\n\n\tif c.keys == nil || time.Since(c.fetchedAt) >= c.ttl {\n\t\tkeys, err := c.fetch()\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t// A stale-but-present cache is preferable to a hard failure\n\t\t\t// on every request the moment one refresh fails (a\n\t\t\t// transient network blip to the issuer's in-cluster\n\t\t\t// Service) — verification with a stale key set still\n\t\t\t// correctly rejects a token signed by a kid that's been\n\t\t\t// fully retired and removed from the real set, it just\n\t\t\t// risks accepting a very recently rotated-in key slightly\n\t\t\t// late, which is not a security regression (the overlap\n\t\t\t// window already tolerates both old and new keys).\n\t\t\tif c.keys != nil {\n\t\t\t\tgoto lookup\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t\tc.keys = keys\n\t\tc.fetchedAt = time.Now()\n\t}\nlookup:\n\tkey, ok := c.keys[kid]\n\tif !ok {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"api: no key found for kid %q\", kid)\n\t}\n\treturn key, nil\n}\n\nfunc (c *JWKSCache) fetch() (map[string]*rsa.PublicKey, error) {\n\tresp, err := c.httpClient.Get(c.url)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"api: fetching JWKS from %s: %w\", c.url, err)\n\t}\n\tdefer func() { _ = resp.Body.Close() }()\n\tif resp.StatusCode != http.StatusOK {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"api: fetching JWKS from %s: status %d\", c.url, resp.StatusCode)\n\t}\n\tvar buf [1 << 20]byte // 1MiB ceiling — a JWKS response is a handful of RSA keys, never this large\n\tn, err := readAllCapped(resp.Body, buf[:])\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"api: reading JWKS response: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\treturn parseJWKS(buf[:n])\n}\n"
}

  "content": "// jwtmiddleware.go is LT-40's core: verify the client-credentials JWT\n// scheme from handoff-03-auth.md v3/v4 and populate AuthContext for\n// LT-39's already-built handlers. This is the actual integration seam\n// with S6's work, not new response-shape design — a verification failure\n// uses the exact same writeUnauthorized this package's handlers already\n// call for a missing AuthContext.\npackage api\n\nimport (\n\t\"errors\"\n\t\"net/http\"\n\t\"strconv\"\n\t\"strings\"\n\n\t\"github.com/golang-jwt/jwt/v5\"\n)\n\n// jwtAlgorithm is the one signing algorithm this verifier ever accepts.\n// Passed explicitly to jwt.WithValidMethods on every parse — never left\n// to the library's default, per handoff-03-auth.md v3's explicit\n// instruction (\"check your JWT library's default behavior — several\n// popular libraries have shipped this vulnerability by not doing this by\n// default\") and RFC 8725 §3.1. This is what makes alg:none and an\n// HMAC-with-the-public-key-as-secret downgrade both fail before the\n// library ever calls KeySource — jwt/v5's WithValidMethods rejects a\n// token whose header alg isn't in this allowlist ahead of signature\n// verification, not as a secondary check after.\nconst jwtAlgorithm = \"RS256\"\n\n// claims is this service's JWT claim set (handoff-03-auth.md v3's\n// minimum: sub, scope, exp, iat, aud, plus iss and kid via the header).\n// RegisteredClaims gives exp/iat/iss/aud validation for free from the\n// library — this verifier still explicitly re-checks Audience below\n// because jwt/v5's built-in audience check (via WithAudience) does not\n// distinguish \"no aud claim at all\" from \"aud present but empty,\" and a\n// token with no audience claim must not silently pass as if it matched.\ntype claims struct {\n\tScope string `json:\"scope\"`\n\tjwt.RegisteredClaims\n}\n\n// jwtVerifier is the collaborator NewJWTMiddleware wraps — an interface\n// so a test can supply a fake instead of a real *JWKSCache doing network\n// I/O, mirroring this package's existing test-double approach\n// (decisions/test-double-strategy.md).\ntype jwtVerifier struct {\n\tkeys     KeySource\n\tissuer   string\n\taudience string\n}\n\n// verify parses and fully validates tokenString, returning the populated\n// AuthContext on success. Every failure returns a generic error — the\n// specific reason (expired, wrong audience, bad signature, unknown kid,\n// disallowed algorithm) is never distinguishable to the caller of this\n// function beyond \"verification failed,\" which is what makes\n// writeUnauthorized's single, undifferentiated 401 body correct here\n// without an extra translation step.\nfunc (v *jwtVerifier) verify(tokenString string) (AuthContext, error) {\n\tvar c claims\n\ttoken, err := jwt.ParseWithClaims(tokenString, &c, func(t *jwt.Token) (any, error) {\n\t\tkid, _ := t.Header[\"kid\"].(string)\n\t\tif kid == \"\" {\n\t\t\treturn nil, errors.New(\"token header has no kid\")\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn v.keys.KeyForKid(kid)\n\t}, jwt.WithValidMethods([]string{jwtAlgorithm}), jwt.WithIssuer(v.issuer))\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn AuthContext{}, err\n\t}\n\tif !token.Valid {\n\t\treturn AuthContext{}, errors.New(\"token invalid\")\n\t}\n\n\t// Audience checked explicitly, not via jwt.WithAudience above: v5's\n\t// audience option treats an all-nil/absent Audience claim\n\t// inconsistently across versions in a way we don't want to depend\n\t// on — asserting it here directly means this check's behavior is\n\t// this file's own to guarantee, not the library's default to trust.\n\tif !hasAudience(c.RegisteredClaims.Audience, v.audience) {\n\t\treturn AuthContext{}, errors.New(\"token audience mismatch\")\n\t}\n\n\tif c.Subject == \"\" {\n\t\treturn AuthContext{}, errors.New(\"token has no sub claim\")\n\t}\n\n\tscope, err := singleScope(c.Scope)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn AuthContext{}, err\n\t}\n\n\treturn AuthContext{Sub: c.Subject, Scope: scope}, nil\n}\n\nfunc hasAudience(aud jwt.ClaimStrings, want string) bool {\n\tfor _, a := range aud {\n\t\tif a == want {\n\t\t\treturn true\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn false\n}\n\n// singleScope requires the token's scope claim to name exactly one\n// recognized scope. handoff-03-auth.md v3 describes the claim as\n// \"space-delimited, values below\" without describing how a handler\n// disambiguates a multi-scope token per request, and AuthContext (LT-39)\n// carries exactly one Scope by design (\"handoff-03-auth.md v3 doesn't\n// describe multi-scope tokens being disambiguated per-request, so one\n// request carries exactly one Scope\"). Rather than guess which of\n// several granted scopes a request is \"really\" using — silently picking\n// one is exactly the kind of authorization ambiguity this project's\n// contracts have consistently ruled against — a token naming zero or\n// more than one recognized scope is rejected outright. This matches the\n// provisioning model in decisions/search-authz-scoping.md: a client is\n// provisioned for the one use case its credential exists for.\nfunc singleScope(raw string) (Scope, error) {\n\tfields := strings.Fields(raw)\n\tvar found []Scope\n\tfor _, f := range fields {\n\t\tswitch Scope(f) {\n\t\tcase ScopeReadOwn, ScopeReadAny, ScopeSearch:\n\t\t\tfound = append(found, Scope(f))\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tswitch len(found) {\n\tcase 0:\n\t\treturn \"\", errors.New(\"token scope claim names no recognized scope\")\n\tcase 1:\n\t\treturn found[0], nil\n\tdefault:\n\t\treturn \"\", errors.New(\"token scope claim names more than one recognized scope, which this verifier does not disambiguate per request\")\n\t}\n}\n\n// NewJWTMiddleware verifies every request's bearer token and populates\n// AuthContext on success, per handoff-03-auth.md v3/v4: public-key-only\n// verification (keys never includes signing-key material — enforced\n// structurally, since KeySource's real implementation, JWKSCache, only\n// ever holds *rsa.PublicKey values parsed from a JWKS response), pinned\n// RS256, kid-based key selection supporting a rotation overlap window (a\n// KeySource backed by the issuer's full published key set, not a single\n// cached key), and issuer/audience checks. Verification failure returns\n// 401 via writeUnauthorized — the same path LT-39's handlers already use\n// for a missing AuthContext, not a new response shape.\nfunc NewJWTMiddleware(keys KeySource, issuer, audience string) func(http.Handler) http.Handler {\n\tv := &jwtVerifier{keys: keys, issuer: issuer, audience: audience}\n\treturn func(next http.Handler) http.Handler {\n\t\treturn http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n\t\t\ttokenString, ok := bearerToken(r)\n\t\t\tif !ok {\n\t\t\t\twriteUnauthorized(w)\n\t\t\t\treturn\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tac, err := v.verify(tokenString)\n\t\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t\twriteUnauthorized(w)\n\t\t\t\treturn\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tr = r.WithContext(WithAuthContext(r.Context(), ac))\n\t\t\tnext.ServeHTTP(w, r)\n\t\t})\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc bearerToken(r *http.Request) (string, bool) {\n\th := r.Header.Get(\"Authorization\")\n\tconst prefix = \"Bearer \"\n\tif !strings.HasPrefix(h, prefix) {\n\t\treturn \"\", false\n\t}\n\ttok := strings.TrimSpace(strings.TrimPrefix(h, prefix))\n\tif tok == \"\" {\n\t\treturn \"\", false\n\t}\n\treturn tok, true\n}\n\n// deadlineBudget is the context.WithTimeout duration this middleware\n// applies to every request before it reaches a handler — the concrete\n// number decisions/context-propagation.md's ruling required (\"this gets\n// a concrete number attached when [the middleware] is written, not left\n// as 'some deadline'\"). Sized to cover realistic p99 DAO latency\n// (single-digit milliseconds for an indexed Postgres/CockroachDB point\n// query or trigram search, per 05-data-ops/research-cockroachdb-postgres-semantics.md)\n// plus this middleware's own JWKS verification path (a cache hit is\n// sub-millisecond; a cache-miss fetch to the issuer's in-cluster Service\n// is bounded by deadlineBudget itself, not a separate timeout, since it\n// happens inside the same request). 5 seconds leaves an order of\n// magnitude of headroom over expected p99 without ever handing the DAO\n// layer a near-zero remaining deadline as a matter of routine operation.\nconst deadlineBudget = 5 * strconv_dummy_seconds\n"
}

Write /Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-worktrees/LT-40/internal/api/stopgap_authorizer.go
input
{
  "file_path": "/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-worktrees/LT-40/internal/api/stopgap_authorizer.go",
  "content": "package api\n\n// StopgapAuthorizer is a TEMPORARY, default-deny Authorizer stand-in —\n// NOT the real caller_referral-backed policy engine (handoff-03-auth.md\n// v3's F8 resolution names that as a later story's own datastore work).\n//\n// Ruled by Marcus Ilori (02), in response to a direct question before\n// this was wired to a live public URL: \"A permissive allow-all\n// Authorizer must never be deployed to a live public URL on a shared\n// production cluster, 'temporary' or not — that's the exact failure this\n// design exists to prevent, and it's a public repo, so being\n// permissive-even-briefly is a permanent public fact.\" Ruling: default-\n// deny, in-memory, hard-coded with a small fixed (sub, allowed record id)\n// map for the seeded QA client credential (LT-51) — enough to\n// demonstrate both the allow and deny paths of read:own's referral check\n// in the live review, without ever allowing an arbitrary caller through.\n//\n// This only affects ScopeReadOwn's referral-membership check.\n// ScopeReadAny's authorization is \"has a valid reason_code,\" already\n// checked in the handler before Authorize() is ever called; ScopeSearch\n// has no per-record target for this Authorizer to gate at all. Both\n// return true unconditionally here — there is no real caller_referral\n// data this stopgap could consult for a real decision on those scopes\n// either way, so denying them would not be more correct, only\n// differently placeholder.\n//\n// A follow-up story replacing this with the real datastore-backed\n// implementation must exist before this is considered load-bearing\n// beyond the live-URL review — flag to the PM if it doesn't.\ntype StopgapAuthorizer struct {\n\t// ReadOwnAllow maps a caller sub to the set of record ids that sub\n\t// may read under ScopeReadOwn — the hard-coded \"referral\" set this\n\t// stopgap stands in for. Empty/nil means the map denies everything,\n\t// which is the safe default for any sub not explicitly seeded here.\n\tReadOwnAllow map[string]map[string]bool\n}\n\nfunc (a *StopgapAuthorizer) Authorize(_ ctxType, sub string, scope Scope, target string) (bool, error) {\n\tswitch scope {\n\tcase ScopeReadOwn:\n\t\tallowed, ok := a.ReadOwnAllow[sub]\n\t\tif !ok {\n\t\t\treturn false, nil\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn allowed[target], nil\n\tcase ScopeReadAny, ScopeSearch:\n\t\treturn true, nil\n\tdefault:\n\t\treturn false, nil\n\t}\n}\n"
}
